, contributes to the singularity, but is factorised at the integrand-level from the hard two-loop subgraph. Clearly, we can associate the colour factor
C_{F}^{2}
with the hard-scattering process.
In analogy to the sub-graphs without ladder structure, we obtain two sets of shift mismatch terms, one for each loop momentum
k
and
q
of the two-loop hard subgraph. The shift mismatch in the “outer” loop, with loop momentum
q
or
q-l
, is proportional to the colour factor
C_{A}C_{F}^{2}/2
, while that of the “inner” loop, with loop momentum
k
or
k-l
, is proportional to

C_{F}C_{A}(C_{F}-C_{A}/2)/2
.
The strategy is to first construct a shift counterterm for the set of non-factorised integrands on the right-hand side of eq. (
116
) and then generalise the expression to the full electroweak amplitude. The result is,

\displaystyle\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,UL)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_diag1_alt}-(q\to q+l)\Bigg)

(117)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_diag2_alt}+\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\left(1-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\right)\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_diag3_alt}\Bigg)-(k\to k+l)\Bigg]\,.

Here, the last diagram, multiplied by the non-standard colour factor

\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}(1-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}})
,
cancels additional non-factorised shift-integrable contributions in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit, coming from the first diagram in square brackets.

The shift counterterm with two-loop crossed ladder subgraphs is again two-loop shift integrable. We will not provide its derivation here, but simply state the result,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,XL)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_diag1_alt}-(q\to q+l)\Bigg)\\
&\quad+\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_diag3_alt}-C_{A}^{2}\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_diag2_alt}\Bigg)-(k\to k+l)\Bigg]\,.\end{split}

(118)

The sum of integrands represented by the last diagram, multiplied by
C_{A}^{2}
in close analogy to the non-ladder type graphs in eq. (
112
), is as usual required to remove non-cancelled
k
-loop shift mismatch terms.

Derivation of the infrared counterterm
\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,3V)}
, which is the last remaining term in eq. (
103
) apart from the ghost contributions, is more intricate since it involves
l\,||\,p_{1}
divergent three-loop graphs with either two triple-gluon vertices or a quartic gluon vertex. Consider the following sum of collinear insertions,

\displaystyle\sum_{\text{insertions}}\includegraphics[width=41.39832pt,valign={c}]{l_p1}\otimes\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_3V_diag1}\to-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_fact}

\displaystyle\quad+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_shift_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_shift_2}\,.

(119)

The first term on the right-hand side is manifestly factorised from the two-loop hard subgraph, this time proportional to the colour factor
C_{A}C_{F}^{2}/2
. Clearly, the other two integrands, shown on the second line, cancel only up to a
simultaneous
shift in both loop momenta,
q\to q+l
and
k\to k-q
.

To obtain the right-hand side of eq. (
119
), we have used, in addition to the by now familiar Ward identities for quark and gluon lines, the quartic-gluon identity shown graphically in figure
4
, which for the example above yields,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_4g_ward_0}=\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_4g_ward_1}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_4g_ward_2}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M2_l_p1_3V_diag1_4g_ward_3}\,.\end{split}

(120)

As usual, we follow the momentum flow convention of section
3.2
. The first and third integrands on the right-hand side are proportional to the colour factor
C_{A}^{2}C_{F}/4
in the sum over attachments in eq. (
119
). The second diagram has vanishing colour factor, which can be proven using,

\displaystyle f^{abc}=-2i\,\text{Tr}{\,\big[\big[t^{a},t^{b}\big],t^{c}\big]}\,,

(121)

and repeated application of the well known Fierz identity,

\displaystyle t^{a}_{ij}t^{a}_{kl}=T_{R}\left(\delta_{il}\delta_{kj}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}\right)\,.

(122)

Using the information above, we can construct a shift counterterm for this class of diagrams as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,3V)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_3V_alt}\\
&\qquad-(q\to q+l\,,\,k\to k-q)\,.\end{split}

(123)

We note the unusual momentum routing of the triple-gluon vertex in the hard two-loop subgraph. Due to the ambiguous choice of loop momentum flow the shift term in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit will look slightly different. However, this ambiguity in the double-collinear region poses no problem otherwise, since the term is cancelled exactly by the shift counterterm in the single-collinear limit already.

To summarise, we have derived local infrared counterterms that remove non-factorising shift mismatches generated by “scalar” contributions in the single-collinear regions. Counterterms are supplemented by a shift in the fermion propagator according to the prescription in eq. (
108
) to avoid double-counting of divergent integrands that would spoil local factorisation. In the double- and mixed-collinear regions limits are applied consecutively and the problem reduces either to the factorisable one-loop case for integrands containing one-loop jet functions, c.f. sec.
7.1
, or the two-loop case, which yield one-loop shift-integrable contributions at worst. In the limit where three loop momenta become collinear the shift integrands factorise and pose no problem to integrability.

For instance, in the region
(1_{l},2_{q},H_{k})
we apply the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit
to the two-loop
l\,||\,p_{1}
-finite remainder
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}(p_{1}+l,p_{2},q,k;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})
, c.f. eq. (
96
), as well as the three-loop shift contribution
\mathcal{M}^{(3)}_{\text{shift}}
.
Shift mismatch terms of the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit yield non-factorisable, one-loop shift-integrable contributions in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit, and the shift counterterm
\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3)}
already guarantees integrability. We note for
q\,||\,p_{2}
collinear insertions on the quark line with momentum
p_{1}+l
we apply the Ward identity for on-shell quark lines, c.f. eq. (
17
), as follows,

\displaystyle=it^{a}t^{b}\,S_{0}(p_{1}+q)\,\not{q}\,S_{0}(p_{1}+l)=it^{a}t^{b}\bigg[S_{0}(p_{1}+l)-S_{0}(p_{1}+l)\frac{\not{p}_{1}\not{l}}{(p_{1}+l)^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_double_collinear_ward}\,,

(124)

where the second term in square brackets vanishes in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit as
\not{p}_{1}\not{l}\simeq-z_{1,l}\not{p}_{1}^{2}=0
. The expression after the second equality is obtained through a simple partial fractioning identity. Thus, the residual shift mismatch in the region
(1_{l},2_{q},H_{k})
can be removed locally using the standard two-loop shift counterterm
{\delta}_{\text{shift},2}
derived in ref.
[
188
]
, up to a collinear factor,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\text{shift},1,2}^{(3)}(p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})\\
&\qquad=\mathcal{C}(z_{1,l},p_{1},l)\bar{v}(p_{2})\widetilde{\delta}_{\text{shift},2}^{(2)}(p_{1}+l,p_{2},q,k;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})u(p_{1})\,,\end{split}

(125)

with
\mathcal{C}(z_{1,l},p_{1},l)
defined in eq. (
97
) and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{\delta}_{\text{shift},2}^{(2)}(p_{1}+l,p_{2},q,k;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_mixed}-(k\to k+q)\,.\end{split}

(126)

Above, the subscript
``1,2"
denotes the order in which collinear limits are applied in the region
(1_{l},2_{q},H_{k})
.

8
Gluon self-energy corrections

In this section, we discuss the treatment of subgraphs containing one and two-loop corrections to the gluon-self energy. The one-loop gluon propagator in Feynman gauge is given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}(k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=89.6981pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L}\\
&\qquad=\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\left[\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{q}+\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{g}+\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{gh}\right]\end{split}

(127)

where
\Pi^{(1)}_{q}
,
\Pi^{(1)}_{g}
and
\Pi^{(1)}_{gh}
denote the quark, gluon and ghost loop contributions, respectively. The gluon tadpole is a scaleless integral and therefore vanishes in dimensional regularisation, so is immediately ignored. We note that we have absorbed one of the gluon propagators
-i/l^{2}
into the definition of
\Pi_{gg}
, which will be a useful convention later. The two-point function is proportional to unity in colour space,

\displaystyle\Pi_{gg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}=\delta^{ab}\,\Pi_{gg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}\,.

(128)

The integrands are given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{q}(k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L_floop}\\
&\qquad=-g_{s}^{2}\,n_{f}T_{F}\,\delta^{ab}\,\frac{\text{Tr}{\,\left[\not{k}\,\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{l}-\not{k}\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right]}}{(l^{2})^{2}k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(129)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{g}(k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L_gloop}\\
&\qquad=-g_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\delta^{ab}\,\frac{C^{\mu\alpha\beta}(l,k-l,-k)C^{\nu}{}_{\beta\alpha}(-l,k,l-k)}{(l^{2})^{2}k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(130)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{gh}(k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L_ghloop_diag1}=-g_{s}^{2}\,C_{A}\,\delta^{ab}\,\frac{k^{\mu}(l-k)^{\nu}}{(l^{2})^{2}k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(131)

The gluon loop contains a symmetry factor of
1/2
. Later, when we investigate one-loop gluon triangle subgraphs, it will be useful to consider an equivalent version of the ghost loop contribution symmetrised under the exchange
k\to l-k
, which we denote by
\Pi^{(1)}_{2,\,sym}
. This can be implemented through a local counterterm
\delta_{gh}
as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{2,\,sym}(k,l)\equiv\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\left[\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{2}(k,l)+\delta_{gh}^{\mu\nu,ab}(k,l)\right]\\
&\qquad=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L_ghloop_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{gg_1L_ghloop_diag2}\Bigg]\\
&\qquad=-g_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\delta^{ab}\,\frac{k^{\mu}(l-k)^{\nu}+(l-k)^{\mu}k^{\nu}}{(l^{2})^{2}k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(132)

with

\displaystyle\delta_{gh}^{\mu\nu,ab}(k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\delta^{ab}\,\frac{k^{\mu}l^{\nu}-k^{\nu}l^{\mu}}{l^{2}k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,,\qquad\int_{k}\,\delta_{gh}(k,l)=0\,.

(133)

Note that
\delta_{gh}^{\mu\nu,ab}
, unlike the symmetrised ghost loop, is not symmetric under the exchange of its Lorentz indices. Equation (
132
) defines the symmetrised one-loop gluon propagator,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Pi_{gg,\,sym}^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}=\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{q}+\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{g}+\Pi^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}_{gh,\,sym}\,.\end{split}

(134)

It is easy to see that the doubled propagator in eq. (
127
) leads to enhanced soft and collinear singularities. Following the discussion in section
3
, in the soft limit
l^{\mu}\to\delta l^{\mu}
,
\delta\to 0
, the integrand of one-loop gluon propagator scales as

\displaystyle\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}(k,l)\sim\delta^{-4}\,,

(135)

for generic non-lightlike values of the loop momentum
k
. The power singularity exhibited in the soft region would suggest we can no longer rely on the leading order approximation for the construction of the counterterm, eqs. (
12
) and (
13
). Similarly, naive power counting in the collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
leads to an apparent linear divergence.
On the collinear pinch surface the polarisation of the virtual gluon with momentum
l^{\mu}
may be proportional to
k^{\mu}
, which is arbitrary and spoils factorisation in the collinear limit.

In ref.
[
166
]
we argued these issues can easily be resolved through a simple Passarino-Veltman tensor reduction, which removes one power of
l^{2}
in the denominator of the photon propagator. Applied to the gluon self-energy, tensor reduction yields,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu,ab}(k,l)&=g_{s}^{2}\delta^{ab}\frac{1}{l^{2}}\left(g^{\mu\nu}-\frac{l^{\mu}l^{\nu}}{l^{2}}\right)\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\int_{k}\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(136)

where we define the
\epsilon
-dependent coefficient,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}=n_{f}\Gamma_{n_{f}}^{\epsilon}+C_{A}\Gamma_{A}^{\epsilon}\,,

(137)

with

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n_{f}}^{\epsilon}=\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{3-2\epsilon}\,,\quad\Gamma_{A}^{\epsilon}=-\frac{5-3\epsilon}{3-2\epsilon}\,.

(138)

As we have argued in ref.
[
166
]
, the longitudinal components in eq. (
136
) lead to a scaleless integral in
l
in the sum over diagrams of the two-loop amplitude. This can be shown through repeated application of the abelian Ward identity, eq. (
15
).
Thus, the problem reduces to the one-loop subtraction, and we make the replacement,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{(2)}_{2}(k,l)\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}(k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\frac{\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,\mathcal{M}^{(1)}(l)\,.\end{split}

(139)

The primed notation
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}
indicates that we have performed a tensor reduction, i.e. the integrated result is the same.
Thus, a suitably modified integrand
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}
can be obtained directly from the corresponding one-loop amplitude
\mathcal{M}^{(1)}(l)
that is free of the problematic behavior in the infrared regions discussed above.
The one-loop amplitude
\mathcal{M}^{(1)}
is rendered finite in the infrared and ultraviolet according to the prescription discussed in refs.
[
166
,
188
]
.
Regularisation of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}
in the
uv
regions is then straightforward, leading to the following expression,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}(k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\left[\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\left(\mathcal{H}^{(1)}(l)+\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\text{singular}}(l)\right)\,.\end{split}

(140)

Above, the term
1/(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}
in round brackets removes the residual UV divergence in the region
k\to\infty
. We note that while the denominator
1/(l-k)^{2}
in fact suppresses any UV divergence in the limit
l\to\infty
it is convenient to make the replacement
\mathcal{M}^{(1)}=\mathcal{H}^{(1)}(l)+\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\text{singular}}
.

Finally, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}(k,l)=\mathcal{H}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}(k,l)+\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2\,\text{singular}}(k,l)\,,

(141)

where
[
166
]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}(k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}-\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right]{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}(l)\,,

(142)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2\,\text{singular}}(k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{(1)}_{\text{singular}}(l)+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}(l)\right]\,.

(143)

The one-loop integrands
{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}_{\text{singular}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}
are defined according to eqs. (
7
) and (
8
) (c.f. section 4.1 of ref.
[
166
]
). The
n_{f}
-contribution
{M}^{\prime\,(2)}_{2}
, as defined by the prescription in eq. (
141
), was calculated recently using numerical techniques in ref.
[
191
]
, for the
nnlo
virtual corrections to the
production of three equal-mass off-shell photons and the
d\bar{d}
-initiated production of a
Z
-boson and two different-mass off-shell photons.

The three-loop QCD amplitude will contain two types of gluon self-energy contributions, which we can construct out of the one- and two-loop amplitudes by considering all possible “insertions” of the self-energy subgraph on the virtual gluon lines.
As for the two-loop electroweak amplitude, we need to perform a tensor reduction of each gluon self-energy subgraph to alleviate the power divergences in the soft and collinear regions. We denote by
\mathcal{M}^{(3)}_{2,a}
contributions which are proportional to the one-loop amplitude after tensor reduction. These include consecutive insertions of two one-loop gluon polarisation tensors, or a two-loop correction on a single gluon line. For this class of diagrams we make the replacement in straightforward analogy to eq. (
139
) and we do not show the explicit result here.

The amplitude
\mathcal{M}^{(3)}_{2,b}
consists of all one-loop gluon self-energy insertions on the two-loop electroweak amplitude.
For the analysis below, we consider the diagrams contributing to the
l\,||\,p_{1}
collinear limit (as usual the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit follows straightforwardly). Below we assign the loop momentum
k
to the gluon polarisation tensor. Consider the decomposition,

\displaystyle\lim_{l\,||\,p_{1}}\mathcal{M}^{(3)}_{2,b}\sim\mathcal{M}^{(3,A)}_{2,b}+\mathcal{M}^{(3,B)}_{2,b}\,.

(144)

The first term on the right-hand side consists of all one-loop gluon self-energy corrections to the gluon line with momentum
l
adjacent to the incoming
p_{1}
quark. Replacing the gluon polarisation tensor by its tensor reduced equivalent, eq. (
136
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(3,A)}_{2,b}\equiv\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_A}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,(3,A)}_{2,b}=ig_{s}^{2}\,\frac{\Gamma^{\epsilon}_{2}}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_A_TR}\,.

(145)

The two-loop amplitude on the right-hand side is rendered locally finite in all infrared and ultraviolet regions according to refs.
[
166
,
188
]
. The residual ultraviolet divergence due to the scalar bubble integrand is removed in the same way as in eq. (
140
).

The amplitude
\mathcal{M}^{(3,B)}_{2,b}
contains gluon self-energy corrections to the loops
q
and
l-q
in diagrams with triple-gluon vertices, as well as corrections to the
q
gluon line in uncrossed and crossed ladder type diagrams, and graphs without ladder structure,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(3,B)}_{2,b}

\displaystyle\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_NL}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_UL}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_XL}

\displaystyle+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_3V_fig2}\,.

(146)

Again, a tensor reduction is unavoidable due to doubled propagators which become problematic in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
region or, in the case of the last set of graphs, in the mixed collinear region
(1_{l},2_{q},H_{k})
. On the other hand, the single-collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
, while being free of power singularities, factorises only when combined with diagrams containing a gluon triangle, which we discuss in the next section. Indeed, the set of graphs with a self-energy correction on the gluon line with momentum
q-l
do not factorise on their own. An additional subtlety involves two-loop jet subgraphs containing a gluon self-energy correction, which, after tensor reduction, require the usual one-loop modifications summarised in section
4.2
.

9
Gluon triangle

Next, we discuss the regularisation and factorisation of one-loop corrections to the QCD three-point subgraph with gluon external states, given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L}\\
&\qquad=\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\left[\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}+\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{g}+\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}+\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{4g}\right]\,,\end{split}

(147)

where we have suppressed the momentum dependence of the functions on the second line. The grey blob represents the sum of quark, gluon and ghost loop contributions, denoted by
\Gamma^{(1)}_{q}
,
\Gamma^{(1)}_{g}
and
\Gamma^{(1)}_{gh}
, respectively. Additionally, we have diagrams with a quartic-gluon vertex, denoted by
\Gamma^{(1)}_{4g}
, which contain a gluon self-energy subgraph. The gluon triangle is proportional to the antisymmetric structure constant
f^{abc}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\gamma,abc}=f^{abc}\,\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\gamma}\,.

(148)

The three-point gluon subgraphs lead to logarithmically divergent contributions to the three-loop amplitude (c.f. figure
5(d)
) in the single collinear regions
(H_{q},H_{k},1_{l})
and
(2_{q},H_{k},H_{l})
, when either
l
is adjacent to the external quark line with momentum
p_{1}
or
q
is adjacent to the incoming antiquark with momentum
p_{2}
. For this reason we find it useful to factor out the gluon propagators
-i/l^{2}
and
-i/q^{2}
in eq. (
147
).

It is well known that the three-gluon vertex satisfies a Ward-Slavnov-Taylor identity to all orders, which relates it to the ghost-gluon vertex
[
204
,
205
,
206
]
. Below, we will show how this identity is implemented locally at the one-loop order, which is the main result of this section. In the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit, where the gluon with loop momentum
l
has longitudinal polarisation, the result is,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg}(q,k,l)+\delta_{ggg,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}(q,k,l)+\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}(q,k,l)\\
&\qquad-ig_{s}\,f^{abc}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg,\,sym}^{(1)\,\nu\rho}(-k,-q)-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg,\,sym}^{(1)\,\nu\rho}(q-k,q-l)\right]\,,\end{split}

(149)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta_{ggg,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}=\delta_{TR,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}+\delta_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}+\delta_{\mathcal{E},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}+\delta_{\Delta\,gh,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}+\delta_{\mathcal{D},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}\,,\end{split}

(150)

where we have suppressed the momentum dependence. Here, we use symmetric integration, equivalent to adding a local infrared counterterm
\delta_{ggg,1}
, to remove locally non-factorisable terms from the triangle integrand in the single-collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
. The functions
\delta_{TR,1}
,
\delta_{\mathcal{U},1}
,
\delta_{\mathcal{E},1}
,
\delta_{\Delta\,gh,1}
and
\delta_{\mathcal{D},1}
are defined later in this section in eqs. (
171
), (
198
), (
202
), (
210
) and (
213
), respectively. Below, we will derive each of the five terms in turn. The integrands
\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}
and
\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}
are defined at the end of this section, shown graphically in eqs. (
217
) and (
219
), respectively.

At the amplitude level, eq. (
149
) can be implemented by replacing the gluon triangle with an equivalent version,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-ig^{\alpha}{}_{\mu}}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg}(q,k,l)\to\frac{-ig^{\alpha}{}_{\mu}}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Delta\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg,1}(q,k,l)=\\
&\qquad\frac{-ig^{\alpha}{}_{\mu}}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg}(q,k,l)+\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\frac{2\eta_{1}^{\alpha}}{d_{1}(-l,\eta_{1})}\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg,1}(q,k,l)\,.\end{split}

(151)

Both sides integrate to the same result given that,

\displaystyle\int_{k}\,\delta_{ggg,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=0\,.

(152)

For clarity, we have written the factor
2\eta_{1}^{\alpha}/d_{1}(-l,\eta_{1})
from the collinear approximation for the propagator
(-ig^{\alpha}{}_{\mu})/l^{2}
, eq. (
12
), multiplying
\delta_{ggg,1}
explicitly. The modified gluon triangle subgraph
\Delta\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}_{ggg,1}
in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
region also requires ultraviolet renormalisation, and we provide the corresponding local subtraction terms throughout this section. We note that the infrared counterterm
\delta^{\nu\rho}_{ggg,1}(q,k,l)/(l^{2}d_{1}(-l,\eta_{1}))
gives a finite contribution to the amplitude for large
l
or
q
but diverges for large values of the “internal” loop momentum
k
. Ultraviolet counterterms are derived according to the discussion in sec.
6
, and in this case they guarantee applicability of the tree-level Ward identities in mixed collinear-UV regions.

To avoid spurious contributions from eq. (
151
) to other collinear regions we introduce a massive fermion propagator to diagrams where
q
is adjacent to the incoming antiquark
p_{2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&iS_{0}(q-p_{2})\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg,1}(q,k,l)\to i\bar{S}_{0}(q-p_{2};l)\,\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg,1}(q,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(153)

based on the prescription used for shift counterterms, eq. (
114
).

In eq. (
149
) and later in this section, we use a subscript
``1"
to denote the Ward identities in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit. For the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit, with
q^{\rho}\simeq z_{2,q}p_{2}^{\rho}
, we have instead,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&q_{\rho}\,\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg}(q,k,l)+\delta_{ggg,2}^{\mu\nu,abc}(q,k,l)=\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\mu\nu,abc}_{qq,2}(q,k,l)+\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\mu\nu,abc}_{\Delta,2}(q,k,l)\\
&\qquad-ig_{s}\,f^{abc}\left[\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg,\,sym}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}(q-k,q-l)-\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\,\Pi_{gg,\,sym}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}(l-k,l)\right]\,,\end{split}

(154)

where this time we label each of the functions on the second line by a subscript
``2"
. It is straightforward to see that the limits are related by a change of variables
p_{1}\to-p_{2}
and, for example,

\displaystyle\delta_{ggg,2}^{\mu\nu}(q,k,l)\equiv\delta_{ggg,1}^{\mu\nu}(l-q,k-q,-q)\,,

(155)

and similarly for
\mathcal{I}_{qq,2}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta,2}
. Analogous to the prescription in eq. (
153
) we introduce a modified fermion propagator
1/(p_{1}+l)^{2}\to 1/[(p_{1}+l)^{2}-q^{2}]
multiplying the counterterm
\delta_{ggg,2}
for graphs where
l
is adjacent to
p_{1}
. Then, we denote by
\Delta\,\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}
the modified integrand that takes into account modifications in both single-collinear regions as follows,

\displaystyle\Delta\,\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}=\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}+\frac{2\eta_{1}^{\mu}}{d_{1}(-l,\eta_{1})}\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{ggg,1}+\frac{2\eta_{2}^{\rho}}{d_{2}(q,\eta_{2})}\delta^{\mu\nu,abc}_{ggg,2}\,,

(156)

in analogy to the shift prescription in eq. (
104
).

Later on, when we investigate the local factorisation of ghost contributions, we will consider two-loop corrections to the
q\bar{q}g
-vertex containing the modified gluon triangle subgraphs
\Delta\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{s}\,\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\Delta\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\rho,c}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_mod}\\
&\qquad=-g_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,t^{c}\,\Delta\,\Gamma_{ggg}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}(q,k,l)\frac{\gamma_{\nu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\mu}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(157)

Here, the limit of interest is where
q
becomes collinear to the incoming antiquark with momentum
p_{2}
. We note that eq. (
157
) diverges in the limit
k\to\infty
, for which we provide appropriate local ultraviolet subtraction terms in this section, but is suppressed by large values of
l
due to the fermion propagator
1/(\not{p}+\not{l})
. The double-UV subtraction term, which renders amplitude-level contributions due to eq. (
157
) finite when two loop momenta become large, is provided in eq. (
374
).

9.1
Fermion loop

We begin with the quark loop contribution to the gluon three-point function, which consists of two distinguishable graphs,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_diag2}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}n_{f}\left(\text{Tr}{\,\left[t^{a}t^{b}t^{c}\right]}-\text{Tr}{\,\left[t^{a}t^{c}t^{b}\right]}\right)\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho}_{q}(q,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(158)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho}_{q}(q,k,l)=-\frac{\text{Tr}{\,\left[\not{k}\gamma^{\mu}(\not{l}-\not{k})\gamma^{\nu}(\not{q}-\not{k})\gamma^{\rho}\right]}}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,.

(159)

The relative sign in the kinematic part in eq. (
158
) is due to the opposite fermion charge flow in the second diagram, while the trace is cyclic. The colour factor can easily be evaluated as,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}{\,\left[t^{a}t^{b}t^{c}\right]}-\text{Tr}{\,\left[t^{a}t^{c}t^{b}\right]}=iT_{F}f^{abc}

(160)

where
we used the well known identity,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}{\,\left[t^{a}t^{b}t^{c}\right]}=\frac{1}{4}d^{abc}+\frac{i}{2}T_{F}f^{abc}\,.

(161)

Here, we have introduced the symmetric structure constant
d^{abc}
,

\displaystyle d^{abc}=2\,\text{Tr}{\,\big[\big\{t^{a},t^{b}\big\}\,t^{c}\big]}\,.

(162)

Clearly, the term proportional to
d^{abc}
cancels in eq. (
160
). We note that the opposite charge flow, the second diagram in eq. (
158
), is related to the crossed version of the first diagram under the simultaneous exchange
q\to k-q
and
k\to l-k
(or by the exchange of the two external vectors
l-q
and
q
).

We are interested in the collinear limit
l\,||\,p_{1}
(the limit
q\,||\,p_{2}
is analogous) where the gluon with loop momentum
l
is adjacent to the incoming quark and has longitudinal polarisation according to eq. (
12
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_diag1_ward}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_diag2_ward}\\
&\qquad=-ig_{s}f^{abc}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{q}(-k,-q)-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{q}(q-k,q-l)\right]\,,\end{split}

(163)

To obtain the second line, we have applied the abelian Ward identity, shown pictorially in figure
2
, and noticed that the result is equal to the difference of two fermion-loop bubble integrals,
\Pi^{(1)}_{q}
defined in eq. (
129
).

Equation (
163
) has the equivalent, suggestive diagrammatic representation,

\displaystyle(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1}

(164)

\displaystyle\quad\to ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{n_{f}}^{\epsilon}\Bigg[\frac{1}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2_TR}-\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1_TR}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\text{longitudinal terms}\,.

On the second line, we have provided its tensor reduced equivalent.
Equation (
163
) cancels against fermion loop corrections to the external
q
and
q-l
loops (contributing to
\mathcal{M}^{(3,B)}_{2,b}
), which we define by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{G}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)=g_{s}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\Bigg[\Pi^{(1)\,\alpha\rho,cd}_{q}(-k,-q)f^{abd}C_{\alpha}{}^{\mu\nu}(-q,l,q-l)\\
&\qquad+\Pi^{(1)\,\alpha\nu,bd}_{q}(q-k,q-l)f^{adc}C_{\alpha}{}^{\rho\mu}(q-l,-q,l)\Bigg]\\
&\qquad\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_gpol_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_gpol_fig2}\,,\end{split}

(165)

After tensor reduction, we obtain,

\displaystyle(-l_{\mu})\,\left[\mathcal{G}^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)+\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q}(q,k,l)\right]

(166)

\displaystyle\quad\to ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{n_{f}}^{\epsilon}\Bigg[\frac{1}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1_TR}-\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2_TR}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg(\frac{1}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\Bigg)\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_gpol_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1_TR}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_floop_gpol_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2_TR}\Bigg)\Bigg]\,.

The result is that fermion loop contributions to
\mathcal{M}^{(3,A)}_{2,b}
, c.f. eq. (
146
), when combined with the triangle graph above, are one-loop factorisable in the single collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
, up to a shift mismatch in both the scalar bubble integrand and the loop momentum
q
.
In the sum of eq. (
166
) the uncancelled longitudinal terms have been neglected, as they lead to scaleless integrals.
As we will see below, both gluon and ghost loop corrections to the one-loop three-gluon vertex yield similar contributions. Factorisation of the ghost terms on the last line of eq. (
166
) can be shown in the same way as for the two-loop amplitude. The only subtlety is that the ghost identity, eq. (
46
), is multiplied by a scalar bubble integrand. The shift mismatch is eliminated from the amplitude by a local infrared counterterm of the form,

\displaystyle\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,\Delta)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=ig_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\frac{\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{(q-k)^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{M3_2_b_B_shift}

\displaystyle\qquad-(k\to k+l,\,q\to q+l)\,,

(167)

which, up to a multiplicative one-loop factor, is the same as the two-loop shift counterterm found in ref.
[
188
]
. Shift subtractions add a contribution to the quark self energy given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Pi^{(2)\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta}(p,q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg[\frac{1}{(q-k)^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_1L_ward_shift_1_nf}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_1L_ward_shift_2_nf}\Bigg]\,,\end{split}

(168)

which is the two-loop analogue of eq. (
43
) with an additional one-loop scalar two-point integrand. The shift counterterm
\Pi^{(2)\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta}
is divergent in the regions
k\to\infty
and
k,l\to\infty
but suppressed in the
l\to\infty
limit.
The single-UV and double-UV singularities are removed, respectively, using the counterterms,

\displaystyle\Pi^{(2)\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta\text{ single-UV}}=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\,\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\text{shift}}_{qq}(p,q,l)\,,

(169)

and

\displaystyle\Pi^{(2)\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta\,\text{double-UV}}=ig_{s}^{2}\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\Bigg\{C_{A}(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}-M^{2}}\Bigg[\frac{4\,l\cdot q\,\not{l}}{(l^{2}-M^{2})^{3}(k^{2}-M^{2})}

(170)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{(l^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{\not{q}}{k^{2}-M^{2}}-\frac{2\,k\cdot q\,\not{l}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\Bigg)\Bigg]-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\text{shift}}_{\text{UV}}(q,l)\Bigg\}\,,

where the one-loop shift counterterms
\Pi^{(1)\,\text{shift}}_{qq}
and
\Pi^{(1)\,\text{shift}}_{\text{UV}}
were defined in eqs. (
39
) and (
65
).

Above, we have demonstrated local factorisation of the (tensor reduced) amplitude
\mathcal{M}^{(3)}_{2,b}
with gluon self-energy subgraphs when combined with the gluon triangle, which is essential for local integrability. As we will see below, we will require a local representation of the gluon loop contribution to the triangle integrand as it combines with diagrams of the
(d3V)
topology in the single-collinear regions. We therefore avoid performing a full tensor reduction of
\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho}_{ggg}
and instead add a local infrared counterterm of the form,

\displaystyle\delta_{TR,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}\,f^{abc}\biggl\{\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\left[\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{gg,\,sym}(-k,-q)-\left(g^{\nu\rho}-\frac{q^{\nu}q^{\rho}}{q^{2}}\right)\frac{i\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\left[\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{gg,\,sym}(q-k,q-l)-\left(g^{\nu\rho}-\frac{(l-q)^{\nu}(l-q)^{\rho}}{(l-q)^{2}}\right)\frac{i\,\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\biggr\}\,,

(171)

which defines the first term on the right-hand side of eq. (
150
).
The function
\delta_{TR,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)
replaces the bubble integrands that are a result of the
l\,||\,p_{1}
Ward identity of the gluon triangle by their tensor reduced versions. Equation (
171
) contributes in the single-UV region as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{TR,1\text{ single-UV}}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}\,f^{abc}\biggl\{\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\left(4\,n_{f}\,T_{F}\,t_{1}^{\nu\rho}(q,k,l)+\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,t_{2}^{\nu\rho}(q,k,l)\right)\\
&\qquad-\frac{\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{(l-q)^{2}}\left(g^{\nu\rho}-\frac{q^{\nu}q^{\rho}}{q^{2}}\right)-\frac{1}{q^{2}}\left(g^{\nu\rho}-\frac{(l-q)^{\nu}(l-q)^{\rho}}{(l-q)^{2}}\right)\right]\biggr\}\,.\end{split}

(172)

The Lorentz tensors
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
appearing on the first line are given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&t_{1}^{\nu\rho}(q,k,l)=g^{\nu\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(q+k-l)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}+\frac{2(k\cdot l)^{2}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)+\frac{2\,l\cdot k\left(k^{\nu}q^{\rho}+k^{\rho}q^{\nu}\right)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\\
&\qquad+2k^{\nu}k^{\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(l-2k)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}-\frac{4\,k\cdot l\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{4}}\right)-\frac{l^{\nu}q^{\rho}+l^{\rho}q^{\nu}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\left(l^{\nu}k^{\rho}+l^{\rho}k^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}+\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(173)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&t_{2}^{\nu\rho}(q,k,l)=g^{\nu\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(2k+3l-8q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}+\frac{4(k\cdot l)^{2}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)+2(3-\epsilon)\frac{l^{\nu}q^{\rho}+l^{\rho}q^{\nu}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\\
&\qquad-8(1-\epsilon)k^{\nu}k^{\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(l-2k)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}-\frac{4\,k\cdot l\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{4}}\right)-\frac{8(1-\epsilon)\,l\cdot k\left(k^{\nu}q^{\rho}+k^{\rho}q^{\nu}\right)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\\
&\qquad-4(1-\epsilon)\left(l^{\nu}k^{\rho}+l^{\rho}k^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}+\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)-\frac{2(1+\epsilon)l^{\nu}l^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(174)

Similarly, the fermion-loop contribution to the triangle subgraph is made finite in the single-UV region by subtracting a local counterterm that matches the divergent behaviour. We write it as follows,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{q\text{ single-UV}}(q,k,l)=4g_{s}^{3}\,n_{f}\,T_{F}\,f^{abc}\frac{1}{(l-q)^{2}}\biggl[4\,k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\rho}\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{4}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\,\frac{k^{\nu}k^{\rho}l^{\mu}+k^{\mu}k^{\rho}l^{\nu}+k^{\mu}k^{\rho}q^{\nu}+k^{\mu}k^{\nu}q^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}-2\,\frac{k\cdot l\,g^{\nu\rho}k^{\mu}+k\cdot(l+q)\,g^{\mu\rho}k^{\nu}+k\cdot q\,g^{\mu\nu}k^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{g^{\nu\rho}(l-q-k)^{\mu}+g^{\mu\rho}(l+q-k)^{\nu}-g^{\mu\nu}(l-q+k)^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\biggr]\,.

(175)

9.2
Ghost loop

Next, we investigate collinear insertions on the ghost-loop contribution
\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}
to the gluon three-point function, given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_diag2}\\
&\qquad=g_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho}_{gh}(q,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(176)

where the kinematic function
\mathcal{K}_{gh}
reads,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{gh}^{\mu\nu\rho}(q,k,l)=\frac{k^{\mu}(q-k)^{\rho}(l-k)^{\nu}+(l-k)^{\mu}(q-k)^{\nu}k^{\rho}}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,.

(177)

In the collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
we use the approximation of eq. (
12
). Thus, we need to consider the contraction of the gluon-ghost-ghost vertex with a longitudinally polarised gluon with loop momentum
-l^{\mu}\simeq z_{1,l}p_{1}^{\mu}
,

\displaystyle\equiv-g_{s}f^{ade}\frac{l\cdot k}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}=g_{s}f^{ade}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{1}{k^{2}}-\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}}-\frac{l^{2}}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{gghgh_ward_cw_RHS_1}-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{gghgh_ward_cw_RHS_2}\Bigg]\,.

(178)

This yields an abelian-like Ward identity, shown graphically on the second line. The term proportional to
l^{2}\sim\mathcal{O}(\lambda)
vanishes in the strict collinear limit
\lambda\to 0
and can safely be neglected in our approximation. Above, we have introduced the new Feynman rule,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ghghgh_QCD}=-ig_{s}f^{dea}\,.

(179)

The sign is determined by moving clockwise around the three-ghost vertex starting from a ghost line whose arrow points
into
the vertex.

Applying eq. (
178
) to the contracted ghost loop,
(-l_{\mu})\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}
, we again write the result in terms of a difference of two self-energy integrands,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}(q,k,l)=-ig_{s}f^{abc}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{{gh},\,sym}(-k,-q)\right.\\
&\qquad\left.-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{{gh},\,sym}(q-k,q-l)\right]+\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\\
&\qquad\equiv\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_acw_ward_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_acw_ward_diag2}-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_cw_ward_diag1}\\
&\qquad-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_ghloop_cw_ward_diag2}\Bigg]\,,\end{split}

(180)

with
\Pi^{(1)}_{{gh},\,sym}
defined in eq. (
132
). Above, we have the remainder
\mathcal{R}_{1}
defined as,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=-ig_{s}^{3}f^{abc}\frac{C_{A}}{4}\frac{1}{{q^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}}\left[\frac{(q-k)^{\nu}l^{\rho}}{(l-k)^{2}}+\frac{(q-k)^{\rho}l^{\nu}}{k^{2}}\right]\,.

(181)

Below, we will show this term cancels against bubble-type contributions to the gluon loop Ward identity.

9.3
Quartic vertex

The three-point function
\Gamma^{(1)}_{4g}
on the second line of eq. (
147
), which contains a single quartic-gluon vertex, explicitly reads,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{4g}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{4g}(q,k,l)\\
&\quad\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_flow2}\,,\end{split}

(182)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{4g}(q,k,l)=f^{cde}\frac{D^{\mu\nu\beta\alpha}_{abde}(l,q-l,k-q,-k)C^{\rho}{}_{\beta\alpha}(-q,k,q-k)}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\\
&\qquad+f^{aed}\frac{D^{\nu\rho\alpha\beta}_{bced}(q-l,-q,k,l-k)C^{\mu}{}_{\alpha\beta}(l,k-l,-k)}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\\
&\qquad+f^{bde}\frac{D^{\rho\mu\beta\alpha}_{cade}(-q,l,k-l,q-k)C^{\nu}{}_{\beta\alpha}(q-l,k-q,l-k)}{(k-q)^{2}(l-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(183)

where the quartic vertex
D^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}_{abcd}
was defined in eq. (
24
).
The factor
1/2
multiplying
\mathcal{K}_{4g}
in eq. (
182
) accounts for the symmetry of the quartic vertex. The “unconventional” momentum routing of the self-energy subgraph of the last diagram in eq. (
182
) (neither gluon line carries momentum
k
) is useful since it allows us to combine divergent integrands in the single-collinear limits with the gluon-loop triangle graph
\Gamma_{g}^{(1)}
.
The second diagram (first diagram) vanishes in the single-collinear limit
l\,||\,p_{1}
(
q\,||\,p_{2}
),

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig2_ward_l_p1}=0\,,\qquad\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_q_p2}=0\,.

(184)

This can be checked straightforwardly by contracting the second term in eq. (
183
) by the collinear momentum
l_{\mu}
. In fact, the scalar and ghost terms contributing to the QCD Ward identity, eq. (
18
), vanish separately. Using the decomposition in eq. (
23
) for quartic-gluon vertices with a longitudinally polarised gluon, the remaining two graphs give,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_LHS}=-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1}+\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]\,,\end{split}

(185)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_LHS}=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1}+\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]\,.\end{split}

(186)

Again, the explicit factors of
1/2
account for the symmetry of the quartic vertex diagram. The first terms on the right-hand sides of eqs. (
185
) and (
186
) are equal to a difference of two gluon self energy graphs,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig1}\\
&\qquad\equiv-ig_{s}f^{abc}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{g}(-k,-q)-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{g}(q-k,q-l)\right]\,,\end{split}

(187)

with
\Pi^{(1)}_{g}
defined in eq. (
130
). In analogy to the fermion loop contribution, eq. (
164
), the integrand in eq. (
187
) factorises up to a shift term when combined with gluon loop corrections to the
q
and
l-q
legs in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit. As we will see below, the other graphs contributing to eqs. (
185
) and (
186
) can be made to vanish locally against similar self-energy contributions to the
l\,||\,p_{1}
collinear limit of the gluon loop.

The quartic vertex contribution to the gluon triangle is made finite in the single-UV region
k\to\infty
by subtracting the following counterterm,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{4g\text{ single-UV}}(q,k,l)=g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{9C_{A}}{4}\,f^{abc}\,\frac{g^{\mu\nu}(2l-q)^{\rho}-g^{\mu\rho}(l+q)^{\nu}+g^{\nu\rho}(2q-l)^{\mu}}{(l-q)^{2}(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(188)

9.4
Gluon loop

Finally, we analyse the gluon-loop contributions, which are more involved. They will have to be summed with both the quartic-vertex integrand and the ghost triangle discussed previously. In addition we will need to introduce a local infrared counterterm that integrates to zero, but removes terms from the gluon triangle integrand that spoil local factorisation. Explicitly, the gluon loop contribution reads,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{g}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop}\\
&\qquad=-g_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\,\frac{-i}{l^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\mathcal{K}^{\mu\nu\rho}_{g}(q,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(189)

with kinematic part,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{g}^{\mu\nu\rho}(q,k,l)=\frac{C^{\mu\alpha\beta}(l,k-l,-k)C_{\epsilon\alpha}{}^{\nu}(k-q,l-k,q-l)C_{\beta}{}^{\epsilon\rho}(k,q-k,-q)}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,.

(190)

In the single-collinear region
l\,||\,p_{1}
we contract the three-point function with
{(-l^{\mu})\simeq z_{1,l}\,p_{1}^{\mu}}
according to the approximation eq. (
12
), and write the result as a sum of four terms as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{g}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_ward_l_p1}\\
&\qquad=\mathcal{B}_{g,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}(q,k,l)+\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}(q,k,l)\,.\end{split}

(191)

Below, we will explain each of the functions on the second line in turn. They are obtained through repeated application of the Ward identity for triple-gluon vertices, shown graphically in figure
3
. The analogous Ward identity in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit can be obtained using eq. (
155
).

Here,
\mathcal{B}_{g,1}
represents all graphs where one of the internal gluon propagators, either
1/k^{2}
or
1/(l-k)^{2}
, has been cancelled, and are therefore similar to two-point bubble insertions, eq. (
130
). We will use symmetric integration in the loop momentum
k
to remove terms that vanish after integration. Graphically, the function
\mathcal{B}_{g,1}
is equal to,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{g,1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_diag1}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_diag1}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_diag2}

\displaystyle\quad+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_diag2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_gh_LHS}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_gh_LHS}\,.

(192)

The first (second) term combines with the second and third graphs on the right-hand side of eq. (
185
) (eq. (
186
)), which are contributions to the
l\,||\,p_{1}
Ward identity due to graphs with a quartic gluon vertex. Their sums simplify to,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_diag1}+\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig1_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]\,,

(193)

and

\displaystyle-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_diag1}+\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_4g_fig3_ward_l_p1_RHS_fig3}\Bigg]\,.

(194)

The sum of integrands shown pictorially in eqs. (
193
) and (
194
), which we denote by the kinematic function
\mathcal{U}_{1}
, is given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{4}f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\left[\frac{C^{\rho}{}_{\alpha\beta}(-q,k,q-k)f^{\nu;\alpha\beta}(l)}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad+\left.\frac{C^{\nu}{}_{\beta\alpha}(q-l,k-q,l-k)f^{\rho;\alpha\beta}(l)}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(195)

The function
f
above is defined as,

\displaystyle f^{\nu;\alpha\beta}(l)=l^{\beta}g^{\alpha\nu}+l^{\alpha}g^{\beta\nu}-2l^{\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\,,

(196)

which is symmetric under exchange of the two Lorentz indices after the semicolon. Interestingly, the first (second) term in square brackets in eq. (
195
) vanishes after performing a symmetrisation under the exchange
k\to q-k
(
q\to q+l-k
). As usual, symmetric integration is implemented using a local infrared counterterm that integrates to zero,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta\,\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\equiv\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\delta_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=0\,,\end{split}

(197)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=-ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{4}\,f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{f^{\rho;}{}_{\alpha\beta}(q-2k)f^{\nu;\alpha\beta}(l)}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad+\left.\frac{f^{\nu;}{}_{\alpha\beta}(q+l-2k)f^{\rho;\alpha\beta}(l)}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(198)

This defines the second contribution to the local gluon triangle counterterm, eq. (
150
). Though
\delta_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\nu\rho}(q,k,l)
integrates to zero, it has a residual UV divergence for large loop momentum
k
. This divergence is removed locally through the counterterm,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{U},1\text{ single-UV}}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=-ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{4}\,f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\biggl[g^{\nu\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(l+2q-4k)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}-\frac{4\,k\cdot l\,k\cdot(l+2q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(5-4\epsilon)\left(l^{\nu}l^{\rho}+l^{\nu}q^{\rho}+l^{\rho}q^{\nu}\right)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}-2(5-4\epsilon)\left(l^{\nu}k^{\rho}+l^{\rho}k^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot q}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-4\,k\cdot l\,\frac{4(1-\epsilon)k^{\nu}l^{\rho}+k^{\rho}l^{\nu}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\biggr]\,.

(199)

Next, the sum of the third and fourth graphs in the definition of the bubble contribution
\mathcal{B}_{g,1}
in eq. (
192
), which we denote by
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, is given by the following integrand,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_diag2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_diag2}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\left[\frac{(q-k)^{\rho}k^{\nu}}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}+\frac{(l-k)^{\rho}(q-k)^{\nu}}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(200)

Again, we notice that the first (second) term in square brackets is symmetric under the exchange
k\to q-k
(
k\to q+l-k
), and symmetric integration yields,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Delta\,\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\equiv\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\delta_{\mathcal{E},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{(q-k)^{\rho}k^{\nu}+(q-k)^{\nu}k^{\rho}}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad+\left.\frac{(l-k)^{\rho}(q-k)^{\nu}+(l-k)^{\nu}(q-k)^{\rho}}{(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\,,\end{split}

(201)

where,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\mathcal{E},1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{k^{\rho}q^{\nu}-k^{\nu}q^{\rho}}{k^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad+\left.\frac{(l-k)^{\nu}q^{\rho}-(l-k)^{\rho}q^{\nu}+k^{\nu}l^{\rho}-k^{\rho}l^{\nu}}{(l-k)^{2}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(202)

The UV divergence for large
k
is cancelled locally using the following counterterm,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\mathcal{E},1\text{ single-UV}}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{4}f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\biggl[\frac{l^{\nu}q^{\rho}-l^{\rho}q^{\nu}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\\
&\qquad-2\left(k^{\nu}q^{\rho}-k^{\rho}q^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{k\cdot(l+2q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(k^{\nu}l^{\rho}-k^{\rho}l^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)\biggr]\,.\end{split}

(203)

Superficially, the last two graphs in eq. (
192
) look similar to the ghost loop contribution to the gluon triangle, eq. (
176
). It can be shown that the sum of these two graphs, which we represent by the function
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)
, is proportional to
(l-q)^{\nu}
or
q^{\rho}
, i.e. one of the “external” gluons has longitudinal polarisation. In principle such terms can be shown to factorise from the hard-scattering tree-amplitude. However, when combined with the newly symmetrised expression in eq. (
201
) and the ghost triangle, eq. (
180
), the result is equal to a difference of two self-energy graphs with a ghost loop, as we will show below. Indeed, if such a singular contribution were to exist, even after integration, it would be in violation of the Ward-Slavnov-Taylor identity.

Explicitly, the sum of integrands reads,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_gh_LHS}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_gh_LHS}\\
&\qquad=-ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{1}+\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{2}}{k^{2}q^{2}(l-k)^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(204)

Here,
\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{2}
denote the numerators of the first and second graphs in eq. (
204
), which read

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{1}&=k\cdot(l-k)(q-k)^{\nu}k^{\rho}\,,\\
\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{2}&=k\cdot(l-k)(l-k)^{\nu}(q-k)^{\rho}\,.\end{split}

(205)

Their sum can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{1}+\mathcal{N}^{\nu\rho}_{2}\equiv k\cdot(l-k)\left[(q-l)^{\nu}k^{\rho}+(l-k)^{\nu}q^{\rho}\right]\,.

(206)

Using the relation

\displaystyle 2k\cdot(l-k)=l^{2}-(l-k)^{2}-k^{2}\,,

(207)

and discarding terms of
\mathcal{O}(l^{2})
, which give a vanishing contribution to the collinear limit
l\,||\,p_{1}
, we obtain,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_gh_RHS_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_cw_gh_RHS_diag2}\\
&\qquad+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_gh_RHS_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_B_acw_gh_RHS_diag2}\Bigg]\,.\end{split}

(208)

Then, when we combine
\Delta\,\mathcal{E}_{1}
, defined in eq. (
201
), with
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and the ghost triangle contribution, eq. (
180
), we obtain,

\displaystyle(-l_{\mu})\,\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh}(q,k,l)+\Delta\,\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\delta_{gh}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)

\displaystyle\qquad=-ig_{s}f^{abc}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{gh,\,sym}(-k,-q)-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\Pi^{(1)\,\nu\rho}_{gh,\,sym}(q-k,q-l)\right]\,.

(209)

Here, we have defined the local infrared counterterm,

\displaystyle\delta_{\Delta\,gh}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=-ig_{s}f^{abc}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{-i}{(l-q)^{2}}\,\delta_{gh}^{\rho\nu}(k,q)-\frac{-i}{q^{2}}\,\delta_{gh}^{\rho\nu}(q-k,q-l)\right]\,.

(210)

The terms
\delta_{gh}^{\rho\nu}(k,q)
and
\delta_{gh}^{\rho\nu}(q-k,q-l)
on the right-hand side are required to symmetrise bubble-type ghost loop contributions under the exchanges
k\to q-k
and
k\to q+l-k
, respectively, according to the prescription given in eq. (
132
). The contributions to the three-loop amplitude in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit given by the right-hand side of eq. (
209
) factorise up to a shift term when combined with corrections to the external legs of the triangle subgraph, following the discussion after eq. (
164
). Again, we require a local counterterm to remove a left-over divergence in eq.(
210
) in the single-UV region, which has the following form,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\Delta\,gh\text{ single-UV}}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{4}\,f^{abc}\frac{1}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\biggl[\frac{q^{\nu}l^{\rho}-q^{\rho}l^{\nu}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\\
&\qquad+2\left(k^{\nu}q^{\rho}-k^{\rho}q^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{k\cdot(l+2q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(l^{\nu}k^{\rho}-l^{\rho}k^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)\biggr]\,.\end{split}

(211)

The term
\mathcal{I}_{qq,1}
on the right-hand side of eq. (
191
) consists of graphs that lead to collinear insertions on the quark line, which can be shown to factorise independently. The function
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta,1}
contains all gluon triangle graphs where one of the “external” propagators
1/q^{2}
or
1/(l-q)^{2}
is cancelled. These terms vanish against ghost contributions to the
q\bar{q}g
-vertex,
(-l_{\mu})\Gamma^{(2)\,\mu}_{qqg}
, either exactly or after a loop-momentum shift. The precise mechanism will be discussed in the next section.

We note that the integrands where the propagator
1/(q-k)^{2}
is cancelled require special treatment through the addition of a local infrared counterterm
\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\mathcal{D},1}
. As we will see, the modified integrand can be distributed among the functions
\mathcal{I}_{qq,1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta,1}
.
To understand this point, we consider the difference,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_acw_diag2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_cw_diag2}\\
&\qquad=-ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{C^{\alpha\nu\rho}(l-k,q-l,k-q)k_{\alpha}+C^{\beta\nu\rho}(k,q-k,-q)(l-k)_{\beta}}{k^{2}q^{2}(l-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\left[\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\left[Q_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)+O_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)\right]+\frac{(l-k)^{\nu}l^{\rho}-k^{\rho}l^{\nu}}{k^{2}q^{2}(l-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad\left.+\frac{g^{\nu\rho}}{q^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}}-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\right)\right]\,,\end{split}

(212)

Above, we recognise the familiar QCD Ward identity, eq. (
18
), up to terms in the integrand that are antisymmetric under the exchange
k\to l-k
. The term on the last line is a difference of two scaleless integrals, which vanish individually after integration over
k
. These terms are an impediment to local factorisation. However, we can remove these contributions altogether through the use of symmetric integration of the bubble integrand
\sim 1/k^{2}(l-k^{2})
, equivalent to adding a counterterm of the form,

\displaystyle\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\mathcal{D},1}(q,k,l)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,l-k,l)-\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)\right]\,,

(213)

which is the last contribution to eq. (
150
).
This yields,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\nu\rho,abc}(q,k,l)+\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\mathcal{D},1}(q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{ggg_2L_ghost_identity_v4}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{1}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\left[Q_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)+O_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)\right]\,.\end{split}

(214)

As in eq. (
46
) symmetrisation of the bubble integrand under
k\to l-k
is implied in the graphical notation on the right-hand side.
The local infrared counterterm
\delta_{\mathcal{D},1}
has the effect of removing terms from eq. (
212
) that are antisymmetric under the exchange
k\to l-k
, and is therefore valid only in the region
l\,||\,p_{1}
. To avoid spurious contributions to the
q\,||\,p_{2}
region we use the prescription in eq. (
153
), with a modified fermion propagator. As usual, the integrand-level modifications corresponding to
\delta_{\mathcal{D},1}
generate additional UV divergences in the region where
k
becomes large. While they integrate to zero, we require a counterterm to remove them locally,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\mathcal{D},1\text{ single-UV}}(q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\biggl[g^{\nu\rho}\left(\frac{l\cdot(l-2k)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}-\frac{4(k\cdot l)^{2}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(k^{\nu}l^{\rho}+k^{\rho}l^{\nu}\right)\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot l}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)-\frac{l^{\nu}l^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\biggr]\,.\end{split}

(215)

The ghost term in eq. (
214
), proportional to
O_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)
, leads to collinear insertions along the fermion line of the three-loop electroweak amplitude. By our definition, it therefore contributes to the function
\mathcal{I}_{qq,1}
on the right-hand side of eq. (
191
), as follows,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}(q,k,l)=\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}(q,k,l)+ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{O_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,.

(216)

We represent the function
\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}
graphically by,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}(q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_cw_diag4}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_acw_diag5}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_acw_diag4}

\displaystyle\quad+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_cw_diag5}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_2L_ghost_identity_RHS_diag3}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_2L_ghost_identity_RHS_diag4}\,.

(217)

Note that this implicitly defines the unmodified function
\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{qq,1}
given by all but the last two terms.

Similarly, we add the contributions from the first term in eq. (
214
) to the function
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta,1}
, which defines the modified integrand,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}(q,k,l)=\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}(q,k,l)+ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}f^{abc}\frac{Q_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)}{k^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\,,

(218)

and is represented by the following sum of graphs,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{I}^{\nu\rho,abc}_{\Delta,1}(q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_cw_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_cw_diag3}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_acw_diag1}

\displaystyle\quad-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ggg_1L_gloop_I_acw_diag3}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_2L_ghost_identity_RHS_diag1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_2L_ghost_identity_RHS_diag2}\,.

(219)

The last two terms denote the scalar contributions proportional to
Q_{0}^{\nu\rho}(l,q)
in eq. (
214
). We have explicitly checked that the sum eqs. (
217
) and (
219
) agrees with the known Ward-Slavnov-Taylor identity.

Finally, the sum of ghost and gluon loop contributions to the gluon triangle is rendered finite in the single-UV region by subtracting the counterterm,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{g\text{ single-UV}}(q,k,l)+\Gamma^{(1)\,\mu\nu\rho,abc}_{gh\text{ single-UV}}(q,k,l)

\displaystyle\qquad=-g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,f^{abc}\frac{1}{(l-q)^{2}}\biggl[16(1-\epsilon)\,k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\rho}\left(\frac{2\,k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{4}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-8(1-\epsilon)\,\frac{k^{\nu}k^{\rho}l^{\mu}+k^{\mu}k^{\rho}l^{\nu}+k^{\mu}k^{\rho}q^{\nu}+k^{\mu}k^{\nu}q^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left(g^{\nu\rho}k^{\mu}+g^{\mu\rho}k^{\nu}+g^{\mu\nu}k^{\rho}\right)\left(\frac{k\cdot(l+q)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}+\frac{1}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4(g^{\mu\nu}l^{\rho}+g^{\nu\rho}q^{\mu})-3\left(g^{\nu\rho}l^{\mu}+g^{\mu\rho}l^{\nu}+g^{\mu\rho}q^{\nu}+g^{\mu\nu}q^{\rho}\right)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2\,k\cdot q\,g^{\nu\rho}k^{\mu}+2\,k\cdot l\,g^{\mu\nu}k^{\rho}}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{3}}\biggr]\,.

(220)

10
Local factorisation for ghost terms

In section
5
we established Ward identities for two-loop corrections to the quark-antiquark-gluon vertex, excluding gluon triangle subgraphs. In the single-collinear regions where the vertex is contracted by the longitudinal polarisation of the external gluon, the result can be expressed in terms of a difference of two-loop quark self-energy corrections plus contributions that cancel by loop momentum shifts and require shift regularisation. Consequently, for general three-loop electroweak amplitudes, scalar contributions to the Ward identities factorise up to shift-integrable Feynman graphs that can be cancelled locally through appropriate shift counterterms, c.f. section
7
.

In this section, we extend this analysis by examining the ghost contributions that appear on the right-hand side of the two-loop QCD Ward identities, and investigate the interplay with modified gluon triangle subgraphs, discussed in section
9
. As we will see below, ghost terms require shift regularisation and factorise locally only up to uncancelled loop polarisation terms, related to ghost self-energy corrections.

In the previous section we developed graphical rules for the gluon triangle in the single-collinear limits. Of interest here are 1) the subgraphs that lead to collinear insertions on the fermion line, represented by the function
\Delta\mathcal{I}_{qq}
, and 2) the integrands
\Delta\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
where one of the gluon lines connecting directly to the fermion line (with momentum
l
or
q-l
) is cancelled by Ward identities. The pictorial representations of these functions are given in eqs. (
217
) and (
219
) for the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit. We will consider each of these contributions in turn below. We repeat that the bubble-type subgraphs
\mathcal{B}_{g}
, where one of the “internal”
k
-dependent propagators is cancelled, are combined with ghost loop contributions and graphs with a quartic gluon vertex, and are regularised separately, c.f. the discussion after eq. (
192
).

The graphs contributing to
\Gamma^{(2)\,\mu}_{qqg}
are all
1pi
, though we will also have to consider diagrams without (two-loop) ladder structure. These involve disjoint insertions of the one-loop subgraphs on the same fermion line, satisfying the one-loop Ward identity, eq. (
38
). In particular, we are interested in diagrams with a three-gluon vertex, which we label by the superscript
(NL-3V)
,

\displaystyle g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,NL-3V)\,\mu,c}(q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_fig1_v2}+\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_fig2_v2}

(221)

\displaystyle\qquad=g_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\,t^{c}\left[S^{(1)}(p+q,k)\frac{i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}W^{(1)\,\mu}(p,q,l)+W^{(1)\,\mu}(p,q,k)\frac{i}{\not{p}}S^{(1)}(p,l)\right]\,,

where
S^{(1)}

and
W^{(1)\,\mu}=Q^{(1)\,\mu}+O^{(1)\,\mu}
were defined in eqs. (
36
)
and (
32
), respectively.
We define the ghost contributions to
\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,NL-3V)\,\mu}
to be,

\displaystyle\begin{split}O^{(2,NL-3V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)&=S^{(1)}(p+q,k)\frac{i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}O^{(1)\,\mu}(p,q,l)\\
&+O^{(1)\,\mu}(p,q,k)\frac{i}{\not{p}}S^{(1)}(p,l)\,,\end{split}

(222)

where we have extracted the terms proportional to
O^{(1)\,\mu}
in eq. (
221
).

We let
O^{(2)\,\mu}
be the sum of ghost terms from two-loop corrections to the fermion propagator involving one or more three-gluon vertices (except the gluon three-point function), defined in appendix
C
. We find it convenient to decompose
O^{(2)\,\mu}
in terms of two colour coefficients as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&O^{(2)\,\mu,c}(p,q,k,l)=g_{s}^{5}t^{c}\frac{i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}\biggl[\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\biggl(O^{(2,NL-3V)\,\mu}+O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}+O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}\biggr)\\
&\qquad+\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\biggl(O^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}-O_{2}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}-O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}\biggr)\biggr]\frac{i}{\not{p}}\,,\end{split}

(223)

where for readability we have suppressed the kinematic dependence on the right-hand side. Here, we consider the limit where the gluon with loop momentum
q
becomes collinear to the incoming anti-quark and apply the approximation of eq. (
13
). Factorisation is guaranteed for both single-collinear regions, except for shift-integrable contributions that can be cancelled using local counterterms, as well as loop polarisation terms which we will discuss at the end of this section.

We contract eq. (
223
) with a longitudinal polarised gluon momentum
q_{\mu}
, and apply the QCD Ward identities, eqs. (
15
), (
18
) and (
45
), which yields,

\displaystyle q_{\mu}O^{(2)\,\mu,c}=\mathcal{O}^{(2)\,c}_{2}-O^{(2)\,c\,\,\text{shift}}_{2}+O^{(2)\,c}_{\Delta,2}+O^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}\,.

(224)

Below, we explain each term on the right-hand side in turn, while their explicit derivation is provided in appendix
E
.

The function
\mathcal{O}^{(2)\,c}_{2}
is analogous to the ghost contribution to the Ward identity at one loop, c.f. eqs. (
45
) - (
47
), consisting of terms where one of the outermost fermion propagators has been cancelled. The resulting expression, which is quite lengthy, is provided in diagrammatic form in eq. (
325
). We analyse the remaining terms on the right-hand side of eq. (
224
) in more detail, since
a priori
they constitute an obstacle to local factorisation.

In ref.
[
188
]
it was shown that in the single-collinear regions ghost contributions factorise independently from the two-loop electroweak amplitude, without extra modifications to the integrand. At three-loop order this is no longer the case, since we have additional non-factorisable shift contributions also for ghost terms, given by,

\displaystyle O^{(2)\,c\,\,\text{shift}}_{2}(p,q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,t^{c}\,\left[\not{l}(\not{p}+\not{l})\left(S^{(1)}(p+l,k)-S^{(1)}(p+l,k-l)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\left(S^{(1)}(p+l,k)-S^{(1)}(p+l,k+q-l)\right)(\not{p}+\not{l})(\not{q}-\not{l})\right]\frac{1}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Biggl[\Biggl(\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}-(k\to k+q-l)\Biggr)

\displaystyle\qquad-\Biggl(\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}-(k\to k-l)\Biggr)\Biggr]\,.

(225)

Clearly,
O^{(2)\,c\,\,\text{shift}}_{2}
vanishes after integrating out the one-loop fermion self-energy subgraph, i.e. it is one-loop shift-integrable in the variable
k
.
However, as for the scalar shift contributions to the Ward identity, this leads to non-factorisable graphs at the amplitude level and need to be subtracted in the
ffs
scheme. We note that the analogous shift mismatch in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit for self energy corrections to the antiquark is related by complex conjugation.

The term
O^{(2)\,c}_{\Delta,2}
in eq. (
223
) has the diagrammatic form,

\displaystyle O^{(2)\,c}_{\Delta,2}(p,q,k,l)=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Biggl[\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_UL-3V_ghost_identity_diag1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_UL-3V_ghost_identity_diag2}\Biggr]+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_2}\,.

(226)

In appendix
E
we show that the contributions of the first two graphs proportional to the colour factor
C_{A}C_{F}/2
cancel against similar graphs from the uncrossed ladder topology, and the remainder is proportional to
C_{A}^{2}/4
. Therefore, we multiply this combination by the non-standard colour factor
C_{A}/2C_{F}
. Equation (
226
) combines with the
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
contributions from the gluon three-point function, defined in eq. (
219
). Their sum cancels up to
additional
non-factorisable one-loop shift-integrable terms, which we denote by
O^{(2)\,c\,\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta,2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&O^{(2)\,c\,\,\text{shift}}_{\Delta,2}(p,q,k,l)=-\left[O^{(2)\,c}_{\Delta,2}(p,q,k,l)-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\Delta\mathcal{I}_{\Delta,2}^{\alpha\beta}(q,k,l)\frac{\gamma_{\beta}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{(p+l)^{2}}\right]\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,t^{c}\,\left[\frac{(\not{l}-\not{k})(\not{p}+\not{k})(\not{l}-\not{q})}{l^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}-\frac{S^{(1)}(p+q,k-q)}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\right.\\
&\qquad-\left.\frac{l_{\mu}W^{(1)\,\mu}(p+l,q-l,k-l)}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\right]-(k\to k+q)\\
&\qquad\equiv\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_UL-3V_ghost_identity_diag1_shift}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1_shift}-\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_2_shift}\\
&\qquad-(k\to k+q)\,.\end{split}

(227)

For the general electroweak amplitude, we again require a prescription to remove non-factorised ghost terms that cancel by loop momentum shifts. However, this do not seem to admit a representation in terms of regular Feynman diagrams in analogy to the discussion in section
7
.
Instead, we remove ghost shift terms directly using the following counterterm,

\displaystyle\delta_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,O)}(p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})=\Biggl\{\Biggl[\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_ghost_diag3}-\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_ghost_diag2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_ghost_diag4}\Biggr]-(k\to k+q)\Biggr\}-\Biggl(\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_ghost_diag1}-(k\to k+l)\Biggr)

\displaystyle\quad+\Biggl(\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{shift_ghost_diag5}-(k\to l-q-k)\Biggr)\,,

(228)

which is the last remaining contribution to eq. (
103
).
Again, the corresponding counterterm for the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit is analogous and we do not show it here. No fermion mass prescription (c.f. eqs. (
108
) and (
153
)) is required in this case.

Next, we investigate the subgraphs contributing to
\mathcal{I}_{qq}
. It can be shown that the two graphs on the last line of eq. (
217
), which correspond to the ghost contributions to the identity in eq. (
214
), factorise independently when we sum over all such diagrams contributing to the relevant single-collinear region. As subgraphs of the two-loop corrections to the
q\bar{q}g
-vertex their contributions read,

\displaystyle-ig_{s}^{5}\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}t^{c}\,\frac{O_{0}^{\mu\nu}(q,l)}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\frac{\gamma_{\beta}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{(p+l)^{2}}\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_LHS_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_LHS_fig2}

(229)

\displaystyle\quad=\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig4}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig3}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig2}\,.

Each collinear insertion leads to two terms according to the abelian Ward identity, eq. (
15
). We remark that the sum of graphs in eq. (
229
) where the fermion propagator
i/(\not{p}+\not{l})
is cancelled is proportional to
\not{q}
, and therefore equivalent to the contraction of the scalar polarised gluon momentum with the
q\bar{q}g
-vertex. This is in complete analogy to the one-loop bubble identity, eq. (
46
). To be specific, we obtain the relation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig3}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_RHS_fig2}=ig_{s}^{5}\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}t^{c}\,\frac{\not{q}}{k^{2}l^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\\
&\qquad=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_id_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_id_fig2}\Bigg]\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_D_ward_id}\end{split}

(230)

This consists of a two-fold symmetrisation of the bubble integrands over their loop momentum flows,
k\to q-k
and
\ell\to q-\ell
.
This identity leads to a factorised integrand at the amplitude-level. Collinear insertions on either side of an electroweak vertex lead to pairwise cancellations of singular integrands. The underlying mechanism of local factorisation is therefore the same as for the one-loop amplitude and we will not explain this further.

10.1
Loop polarisations in ghost terms

Next, consider the class of diagrams discussed in section
7.1
