, involving a (modified) one-loop
p_{1}
jet function in the region
(1_{l},H_{k},H_{q})
with an outgoing longitudinal gluon with momentum
l
, connecting to a triple-gluon vertex. Factorisation of ghost terms follows directly from application of the one-loop Ward identities, eqs. (
18
) and (
46
), and is, up to the quark jet function, equivalent to factorisation of ghost terms for the two-loop amplitude (c.f. section 5.3 in ref.
[
188
]
),

\displaystyle\left.\lim_{l\to-z_{1,l}p_{1}}\,\frac{-ig_{\mu\nu}}{l^{2}}\,v(p_{2})\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(p_{1}+l,p_{2},q;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})\frac{(\not{p}_{1}+\not{l})}{(p_{1}+l)^{2}}\Delta\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\nu,c}(p_{1},l,k)u(p_{1})\right|_{\text{ghost}}

\displaystyle\quad=-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ghost_jet_p1_diag2}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{ghost_jet_p1_diag3}+\text{ finite}\,.

(231)

Terms where the
1/(\not{p}_{1}+\not{l})
quark propagator is cancelled are not divergent in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit and are not explicitly shown on the right-hand side. Modifications to the one-loop quark jet function, as summarised in section
4.2
, eliminate non-factorising loop polarisation terms from the jet subgraph.

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_I_ward_NF_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_I_ward_NF_2}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_I_ward_NF_3}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{ggg_I_ward_NF_4}

Figure 12
:
Subgraphs
\Gamma^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}
originating from the
q\,||\,p_{2}
Ward identity that yield locally non-factorisable loop polarisation terms.

Now, we analyse the remaining contributions to eq. (
217
), which factorise up to loop polarisation terms that cannot be removed by symmetric integration. The problematic subgraphs in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
region are shown in figure
12
, and we denote the corresponding integrand by
\Gamma^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}
. Explicitly, we have,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}(p,q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,t^{c}\,\frac{1}{(l-k)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\frac{4\not{k}-\not{q}}{l^{2}k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}-\frac{2(\not{l}-\not{q})}{l^{2}k^{2}(l-q)^{2}}

(232)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3\not{q}-4\not{k}}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}+\frac{2\not{l}}{l^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}-\frac{\not{q}q^{2}}{l^{2}k^{2}(q-k)^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\Bigg]\,.

To obtain the right-hand side we have written scalar products in the numerator in terms of the inverse propagators of the triangle integrand. The last term in square brackets is of
\mathcal{O}(q^{2})
and therefore finite in the
q\,||\,p_{2}
limit, which means we can safely ignore it. Terms proportional to
\not{q}
are unproblematic, since they are equivalent to a contraction of the longitudinal polarisation vector
q^{\mu}
at the
q\bar{q}g
vertex and factorise in analogy to the identity (
46
). However, terms proportional to
\not{l}
and
\not{k}
spoil local factorisation since they correspond to an arbitrary polarisation.

Similarly non-factorising subgraphs appear on the right-hand side of eq. (
224
), denoted by
O^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}
and defined by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&O^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}(p,q,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag1_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag2_2}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{3}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,t^{c}\left[\frac{(\not{q}-\not{l})\,\widetilde{\Pi}(q-l,-k)}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}}+\frac{\not{k}\,\widetilde{\Pi}(k,l)}{k^{2}(q-k)^{2}}\right]\,,\end{split}

(233)

where the symmetrised ghost self-energy
\widetilde{\Pi}
was defined in eq. (
48
). Again, these graphs constitute locally non-factorisable contributions and cannot be removed using symmetric integration due to different momentum dependence in the ghost self-energy subgraphs. Shifting the first term in square brackets in eq. (
233
) by
k\to k-q
and combining eqs. (
233
) and (
232
) one can show that the uncancelled non-factorising part is proportional to a difference of two bubble integrands. We note that these terms also spoil collinear factorisation in the double-UV region, where
k
and
l
correspond to large loop momenta but
q
has scalar polarisation.

11
Conclusions

In this paper, we have made crucial steps in applying the local factorisation framework developed in refs.
[
166
,
188
]
for the first time to the production of multiple off-shell electroweak bosons in quark-antiquark scattering at the three-loop order. A key achievement has been the derivation of local Ward identities for both the two-loop corrections to the quark-antiquark-gluon vertex and the one-loop gluon triangle subgraph, which constitute some of the main results of this paper.

Ward identities are the key mechanism by which local factorisation is achieved in the
ffs
method. Through the systematic use of Ward identities on the amplitude integrand, infrared singularities are shown to factorise from a hard-scattering function containing the dependence on the mass-scales and momenta of the final-state electroweak bosons. This enables the use of the simplest process, the
2\to 1
form factor, to remove initial-state infrared singularities directly from the loop integrand. All counterterms contain quadratic denominators, so that the resulting integrals can be deformed in the complex plane to avoid threshold singularities. Moreover, we have established how the gluon three-point subgraph connects with other two-loop corrections to quark-antiquark-gluon vertex through Ward identities. In addition, we have derived Ward identity-preserving ultraviolet counterterms for two-loop Green’s functions, which are a crucial element in maintaining integrability in mixed UV-collinear regions, a necessary step for managing the interplay of singularities at three loops and beyond.

As has been shown in ref.
[
188
]
, the triple-gluon vertex contracted with a scalar polarisation of an external gluon can be written as a sum of two scalar terms (for which a hard propagator is cancelled) and two ghost-gluon vertices multiplied by a momentum vector each. We have generalised this to the one-loop gluon three-point function, which is equivalent to a local implementation of the well known Ward-Slavnov-Taylor identities. Using local infrared counterterms that correspond to symmetric integration of the gluon triangle in the single-collinear regions, we were able to 1) combine bubble-type integrands with one-loop corrections to the external legs, and 2) remove locally non-factorising contributions from the gluon triangle subgraph.

At three loops, the amplitude exhibits locally non-factorising contributions of two kinds: those that cancel after individual loop momentum shifts and those involving virtual gluon momenta that do not satisfy local Ward identities. To address the former, we have developed local infrared counterterms for both the amplitude and the form factor subtraction terms, in a non-trivial extension of the approach introduced ref.
[
188
]
. The challenge was to avoid spurious contributions of counterterms in different collinear regions, made possible by the introduction of a fictitious fermion propagator with a judicious choice of mass regulator. The shift counterterms are a critical component of the
ffs
approach and underscore its versatility at higher loops. In particular, we have derived new two-loop shift-integrable counterterms that cancel shift mismatches in two loop momentum variables. The counterterms for scalar contributions to shifted integrands are represented in terms of standard Feynman diagrams multiplied by non-standard colour factors. We have also identified familiar one-loop factorisable and shift-integrable terms in diagrams containing a regularised one-loop quark jet function. Additionally, we have encountered for the first time shift integrands due to ghosts contributions to the Ward identities propagating in the loop. We note that the corresponding shift counterterms, which eliminate these terms directly from the three-loop integrand, do not allow a representation in terms of regular Feynman diagrams.

While the introduction of zero integrals via integrand symmetrisation and shift terms — required to enable local Ward identity cancellations —
does not pose a conceptual issue, it is expected to impact numerical implementations. In particular, these additional terms may increase the evaluation time of integrands and potentially affect numerical stability due to large local cancellations.
We aim to address these challenges in future work.

Another significant challenge we encountered is the appearance of loop polarisation terms in ghost contributions to single-collinear regions.
At two loops ghost contributions factorise separately and are cancelled by the corresponding form factor counterterms containing a triple-gluon vertex. However, at three loops the gluon triangle subgraph, combined with subgraphs containing two triple-gluon vertices, introduces non-factorised ghost contributions that we could not eliminate using symmetric integration.

Loop polarisations also occur when a virtual collinear gluon connects to a jet subgraph. While counterterms have been developed for two-loop QED corrections in electron-positron annihilation
[
166
]
and later for QCD corrections in quark-antiquark scattering
[
188
]
, extending this procedure to the two-loop quark jet function at three loops remains essential. Moreover, similar generalisations will be critical for the gluon jet function, which plays a key role in other phenomenologically significant processes, such as electroweak production through gluon fusion near the quark pair production threshold and Higgs production at two loops in
heft
. In addition, the expectation is that the methods developed in this paper - including the two-loop Ward identities and modifications to the gluon triangle subgraph - can, in future work, be applied to processes with colourful final states.

Loop polarisations present a significant challenge to the local subtraction program, and whether the standard symmetrisation approach is valid for more complex processes remains an open question. Since these issues originate from the momentum-space integral representation of the scattering amplitude, exploring alternative formulations, such as Schwinger, Feynman-parameter, or Mellin-Barnes representations, may provide new insights, albeit at the cost of additional integrations over auxiliary variables and potentially obscuring gauge symmetries as well as complicating numerical integration. However, we anticipate that further refinements in our approach will help address these challenges in future work.

In summary, the techniques introduced in this work, including three-loop shift counterterms, local modifications to the gluon triangle subgraph, and the derivation of two-loop Ward identities, lay the groundwork for extending the approach beyond two loop electroweak amplitudes, while resolving the remaining challenges involving loop polarisation terms will be crucial for completing the local subtraction framework at three loops.

Appendix A
Collinear approximations

To show local factorisation, we perform a light-cone approximation of the metric tensor with respect to the direction specified by the momentum
k^{\mu}
of the virtual electroweak boson or gluon, which becomes on-shell on the collinear
ps
(
ps
)
[
166
]
,

g^{\mu\nu}=\frac{k^{\mu}\eta_{i}^{\nu}}{k\cdot\eta_{i}}+\frac{k^{\nu}\eta_{i}^{\mu}}{k\cdot\eta_{i}}-\frac{\eta_{i}^{2}k^{\mu}k^{\nu}+k^{2}\eta_{i}^{\mu}\eta_{i}^{\nu}}{(k\cdot\eta_{i})^{2}}+g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\,,\quad i=1,2\,,

(234)

where
\eta_{i}
is an auxiliary vector chosen to have a large rapidity separation from
p_{1}
(
p_{2}
) in the collinear limit
k\,||\,p_{1}
(
k\,||\,p_{2}
), with
g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}=g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\eta_{i\,\nu}=0
, to avoid producing additional pinches. Since the virtual momentum goes on-shell in either collinear limit, we can drop the term proportional to
k^{2}=0
in eq. (
234
) without affecting the collinear behavior of the amplitude. In ref.
[
166
]
we found it is useful to introduce an alternative version of eq. (
234
) containing quadratic, instead of linear, denominators
10
10

10

The choice
\eta_{1}=p_{2}
(
\eta_{2}=p_{1}
) allows us to combine the integrands valid in the collinear regions
k\,||\,p_{1}
(
k\,||\,p_{2}
) with the soft approximation into a single form factor subtraction term, c.f. sec. 3.1 of ref.
[
166
]
.
,

g^{\mu\nu}=\frac{2k^{\mu}\eta_{i}^{\nu}}{d_{i}(k,\eta_{i})}+\frac{2k^{\nu}\eta_{i}^{\mu}}{d_{i}(k,\eta_{i})}-\frac{4\eta_{i}^{2}k^{\mu}k^{\nu}}{d_{i}^{2}(k,\eta_{i})}+g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\,,\quad i=1,2\,,

(235)

with

\displaystyle d_{1}(k,\eta_{1})=-(k-\eta_{1})^{2}+\eta_{1}^{2}\,,\quad d_{2}(k,\eta_{2})=(k+\eta_{2})^{2}-\eta_{2}^{2}\,.

(236)

We note that eqs. (
234
) and (
235
) agree in the strict collinear limits, where
k^{2}=0
. Similarly, for the jet-line momentum
k
we perform a light-cone decomposition with respect to the collinear directions specified by the external momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}=z_{i,k}\,p_{i}^{\mu}+k_{\perp i}^{\mu}+\beta_{i,k}\,\eta_{i}^{\prime\mu}\,,\quad 0<z_{i,k}<1\,,\quad i=1,2\,,

(237)

with

\displaystyle z_{i,k}=\frac{k\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{i}}{\eta^{\prime}_{i}\cdot p_{i}}\,,\quad\beta_{i,k}=\frac{k\cdot p_{i}}{\eta^{\prime}_{i}\cdot p_{i}}\,.

(238)

Above,
\eta^{\prime}_{i}
is an auxiliary light-like vector,
z_{i}
is the momentum fraction of the jet-line in the direction of the external on-shell momentum
p_{i}
and
k_{\perp i}
determines the transverse distance from the pinch singularity,
k_{\perp i}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{i}=0
and
k_{\perp i}\cdot p_{i}^{\mu}=0
. We note that in general
\eta^{\prime}_{i}
is not related to the auxiliary vector
\eta_{i}
in eq. (
234
) although in practice we set
\eta^{\prime}_{1}=\eta_{1}=p_{2}
and
\eta^{\prime}_{2}=\eta_{2}=p_{1}
.

To identify the strength of the pinch singularities, we perform some elementary power counting by rescaling the components of soft and collinear momenta by a dimensionless parameter
\lambda
and extracting the leading divergent behaviour of the amplitude in the limit
\lambda\to 0
. Soft lines have momenta that are vanishing in all four components, and scale as,

\displaystyle\ell_{j}\sim\lambda Q\,,

(239)

where
Q=\sqrt{s_{12}}
. Since a pinch surface
S
is specified by algebraic conditions on loop momenta, it can be understood as an algebraic surface embedded within the loop momentum space of the Feynman graph
[
199
]
. Therefore, it is useful to distinguish between
intrinsic coordinates
, which parametrise the pinch surface, and
normal coordinates
, which determine deviations from the pinch surface. For a soft divergence, the
ps
has dimension zero, i.e. is given by a point in loop momentum space, and the normal coordinates are given by the components
\ell_{j}^{\mu}
. The components
z_{i,k}\,p_{i}^{\mu}
correspond to points on the collinear
ps
. The jet line components scale as,

\displaystyle\beta_{i,k}\sim\lambda Q\,,\quad k_{\perp i}\sim\sqrt{\lambda}Q\,.

(240)

To leading order in the limit
k\,||\,p_{i}
, we can approximate any vector
v^{\mu}(p_{i},z_{i,k})
in the direction of
p_{i}
by,

\displaystyle v^{\mu}(p_{i},z_{i,k})\sim\frac{v\cdot\eta_{i}}{d_{i}(k,\eta_{i})}k^{\mu}=f(z_{i,k})p_{i}^{\mu}+\mathcal{O}{(\sqrt{\lambda})}\,.

(241)

Again, we have replaced linear denominators for quadratic ones,
\eta_{i}\cdot k\to d_{i}
.
Together, eqs. (
235
) and (
241
) produce the correct behaviour in the corresponding collinear limits
k\,||\,p_{1}
or
k\,||\,p_{2}
.

Appendix B
Counterterms for the one-loop jet function

At one-loop, the quark jet function reads,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(p_{1},k,l)u(p_{1})=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{jet_1L_XL}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{jet_1L_3V}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{jet_1L_UL}

\displaystyle\qquad=g_{s}^{3}\,t^{c}\bigg[\left(\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right)V^{(1)\,\mu}(p_{1},k,l)+\frac{C_{A}}{2}W^{(1)\,\mu}(p_{1},k,l)

(242)

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{F}N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)\gamma^{\mu}\bigg]u(p_{1})\,,

where we define
N_{S}^{(1)}
to include the adjacent quark propagator,

\displaystyle N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)=S^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)\frac{\not{p}_{1}+\not{k}}{(p_{1}+k)^{2}}\,.

(243)

In the definition of the one-loop jet function we exclude the quark propagator with momentum
p_{1}+k
. The one-loop two- and three-point functions
V^{(1)\,\mu}
,
W^{(1)\,\mu}
and
S^{(1)}
were defined in eqs. (
29
), (
32
) and (
36
), respectively. At the integrand level, the quark self energy
N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)
has an enhanced collinear divergence in the region
(1_{k},H_{l})
due to an additional power of the quark propagator with momentum
p_{1}+k
. Though this integrates to factorisable form, it spoils manifest local factorisation. As a consequence we can no longer use the collinear approximation of eq. (
12
), which is only valid at leading order in the collinear expansion, eq. (
237
). As has been shown in ref.
[
188
]
this can be remedied by making the replacement,

\displaystyle N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)\to\Delta N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)=\frac{(1-\epsilon)}{(p_{1}+k+l)^{2}l^{2}}\,.

(244)

where

\displaystyle\int_{l}\,\left[N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)-\Delta N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)\right]=0\,.

(245)

The modified quark self-energy
\Delta N_{S}^{(1)}
is obtained by symmetrising under the loop-momentum exchange
l\to-(p_{1}+k+l)
, which is equivalent to adding a counterterm

\delta_{S,1}
,

\displaystyle\Delta N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)=N_{S}^{(1)}(p_{1}+k,l)+\delta_{S,1}(p_{1},k,l)\,,

(246)

with

\displaystyle\delta_{S,1}(p_{1},k,l)=-(1-\epsilon)\frac{(\not{p}_{1}+\not{k}+2\not{l})(\not{p}_{1}+\not{k})}{(p_{1}+k+l)^{2}l^{2}(p_{1}+k)^{2}}\,,\quad\int_{l}\,\delta_{S,1}=0\,.

(247)

Adopting the language of ref.
[
188
]
, we shall refer such modifications as
symmetric integration
. Both the original and the modified quark self-energy function is finite in the region
(1_{l},H_{k})
.

The modified quark jet function, which cancels the
k\,||\,p_{1}
singularity and is free of loop polarisations in this region, reads,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Delta\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(p_{1},k,l)=\left.\left[\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\mu,c}+C_{F}\,\delta_{S,1}\gamma^{\mu}+\delta_{\perp,1}^{\mu,c}+\delta_{\mathcal{J},1}^{\mu,c}\right]\right.|_{\eta^{\prime}_{1}=p_{2}}\,,\end{split}

(248)

where for legibility we have suppressed the kinematic dependence of the functions on the right-hand side. The local infrared counterterms
\delta_{\perp,1}
and
\delta_{\mathcal{J},1}
remove loop polarisation terms from the one-loop jet integrand with,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\perp,1}^{\mu,c}(p_{1},k,l)=-g_{s}^{2}\,t^{c}\,\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{l^{2}(p_{1}+l)^{2}}\not{l}_{\perp}\bigg[C_{F}\,\frac{(p_{1}+l)^{\mu}}{(p_{1}+k+l)^{2}}\\
&\qquad-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\frac{2l^{\mu}}{(l-k)^{2}}+(l\to\tilde{l}\,)\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(249)

and
11
11

11

Due to a sign error in the first line of eq. (4.33) of ref.
[
188
]
the term
\delta\mathcal{J}^{\mu}(p_{1},k,-l-p_{1})
multiplying
C_{A}
in eq. (
250
) has opposite sign.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\delta_{\mathcal{J},1}^{\mu,c}(p_{1},k,l)=g_{s}^{3}\,t^{c}\,\bigg[\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[{V}_{k}(k,-l-p_{1})-{V}_{k}(k,l)\right]\\
&\qquad+C_{A}\,\delta\mathcal{J}^{\mu}(p_{1},k,-l-p_{1})-C_{F}\,\delta\mathcal{J}^{\mu}(p_{1},k,l)\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(250)

where

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{J}^{\mu}(p_{1},k,l)=\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{(p_{1}+l+k)^{2}}\left(\frac{2l^{\mu}+p_{1}^{\mu}+k^{\mu}}{l^{2}}-\frac{2(p_{1}+l)^{\mu}+k^{\mu}}{(p_{1}+l)^{2}}\right)\frac{\not{\eta}^{\prime}_{1}}{2p_{1}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{1}}\,,

(251)

and
V_{k}^{(1)\,\mu}
is the part of the the one-loop vertex function
V^{(1)}
, defined in eq. (
55
), that produces a divergence when
l
is hard and
k
becomes collinear to
p_{1}
,

\displaystyle V_{k}^{(1)}(p_{1},k,l)=-\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{(p_{1}+l+k)^{2}}\left[\frac{2(p_{1}+l)^{\mu}\not{l}}{l^{2}(p_{1}+l^{2})}-\frac{\gamma^{\mu}}{l^{2}}\right]\,.

(252)

Here, we (implicitly) perform a light-cone decomposition of the loop momentum
l^{\mu}
and the Dirac matrix
\not{l}
in the
p_{1}
-direction according to eq. (
237
). Finiteness in the region
(1_{k},H_{l})
can be shown by decomposing also the Dirac matrix
\gamma^{\mu}
into light-cone components as follows,

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}=\frac{p_{1}^{\mu}\not{\eta}^{\prime}_{1}}{p_{1}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{1}}+\frac{\eta_{1}^{\prime\mu}\not{p}_{1}}{p_{1}\cdot\eta^{\prime}_{1}}+\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\,,

(253)

with
\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\,p_{1\,\mu}=\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\,\eta_{1\,\mu}^{\prime}=0
.
The vector
\tilde{l}^{\mu}
on the right-hand side of eq. (
249
) is equivalent to
l^{\mu}
up to a reflection on the transverse plane
l_{\perp}\to-l_{\perp}
. The choice
\eta_{1}^{\prime}=p_{2}
guarantees finiteness in the region
(2_{k},H_{l})
.

Counterterms for the
p_{1}
jet function do not affect the
l\,||\,p_{1}
region, which factorises (up to shift-integrable terms) and whose singularity is removed by a form-factor counterterm. Diagrams where the gluon line with momentum
k
directly attaches to the incoming anti-quark leg also exhibit a collinear divergence in the limit
k\,||\,p_{2}
. Application of the QCD Ward identities on the (UV-regulated) quark jet integrand in the region
(2_{k},H_{l})
produces a shift-mismatch, proportional to a (renormalised) on-shell one-loop self-energy correction. Though this shift-mismatch is equivalent to a scaleless integral which vanishes in dimensional regularisation, it spoils local factorisation.
By construction, the jet counterterms eliminate this shift-mismatch locally, i.e. the modified jet integrand vanishes when contracted by
k^{\mu}\simeq z_{k,2}p_{2}^{\mu}
, except for non-abelian terms
O^{\mu}
which generate the whole singularity in the region
(2_{k},H_{l})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k_{\mu}\,\Delta\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(p_{1},k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{jet_mod_p1_ward}\\
&\qquad\underset{k\,\simeq\,z_{k,2}p_{2}}{\to}\includegraphics[width=82.8019pt,valign={c}]{jet_mod_p1_ward_diag1}+\includegraphics[width=82.8019pt,valign={c}]{jet_mod_p1_ward_diag2}\,.\end{split}

(254)

As has been shown in ref.
[
188
]
the sum of all ghost terms factorise in the two-loop amplitude.

For the analogous case for the
p_{2}
jet function, which exhibits loop polarisation terms in the region
(2_{k},H_{l})
, the counterterms are obtained by a change of variables
p_{1}\to-p_{2}
and
\eta^{\prime}_{1}\to\eta^{\prime}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{J}_{2}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(p_{2},k,l)=\left.\Delta\mathcal{J}_{1}^{(1)\,\mu,c}(-p_{2},k,l)\right|_{\eta^{\prime}_{2}=p_{1}}\,.

(255)

Appendix C
Two-loop two- and three-point Green’s functions

The uncrossed ladder contribution to the two-loop
qcd
corrections to the quark self-energy, eq. (
67
) reads,

\displaystyle\Pi_{qq}^{(2,UL)}=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_UL}=ig_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}^{2}S^{(2,UL)}(p,k,l)\,,

(256)

where

\displaystyle S^{(2,UL)}(p,k,l)=2(1-\epsilon)\frac{(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})}{l^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}}\,.

(257)

The crossed ladder contribution is given by,

\displaystyle\Pi_{qq}^{(2,XL)}=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_XL}=ig_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}\,\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)S^{(2,XL)}(p,k,l)\,,

(258)

with

\displaystyle S^{(2,XL)}(p,k,l)=\frac{\gamma_{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{k})V^{(1)\,\mu}(p,k,l)}{k^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\,.

(259)

Finally, the three-gluon vertex contribution reads

\displaystyle\Pi_{qq}^{(2,3V)}=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_3V}=-ig_{s}^{4}\,\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\,S^{(2,3V)}(p,k,l)\,,

(260)

with kinematic part

\displaystyle S^{(2,3V)}(p,k,l)=\frac{\gamma_{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{k})W^{(1)\,\mu}(p,k,l)}{k^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\,,

(261)

which has the same structure as eq. (
259
).

Similar to the self-energy graphs, we find it useful to split the
qq\gamma
-vertex into crossed and uncrossed ladder and three-gluon vertex contributions,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2)\,\mu}=\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}+\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,XL)\,\mu}+\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,3V)\,\mu}\,.

(262)

The integrand for the uncrossed ladder contribution takes the form,

\displaystyle e\,\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_UL_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_UL_fig3}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_UL_fig1}

\displaystyle\qquad=ie\,g_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}^{2}\,V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)\,,

(263)

with
V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}V_{i}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}
. The individual kinematic vertex functions are given explicitly by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}&=-\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})S^{(1)}(p+l+q,k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}((p+l+q)^{2})^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\end{split}\,,

(264)

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}&=-\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}}\end{split}\,,

(265)

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{3}&=\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})V^{(1)\,\mu}(p+l,q,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(266)

where for the sake of readability we have suppressed the arguments on the left-hand side.
The crossed ladder diagrams are,

\displaystyle e\,\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,XL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_XL_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_XL_fig3}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_XL_fig1}

\displaystyle\qquad=ie\,g_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)\,,

(267)

with
V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}V_{i}^{(2,XL)\,\mu}
, where

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1}&=\frac{V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+l+q,-l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\end{split}\,,

(268)

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}&=\frac{\gamma_{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,l,k)}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(269)

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{3}&=\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{k}+\not{q})\gamma^{\beta}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{k}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{k}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\beta}}{k^{2}l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+k+l)^{2}(p+k+l+q)^{2}(p+k+q)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(270)

We emphasise that the second diagram on the right-hand side of eq. (
267
), corresponding to the kinematic function
V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}
defined in eq. (
269
), has a different momentum routing compared to the others. This proves a convenient choice for the single-UV region. However, the entire three-loop amplitude is symmetrised over the virtual gluon momenta, and we will present explicitly symmetrised versions of all double-UV counterterms.
This is especially useful for non-planar topologies, and lead to simplified numerators in the double-UV region.

Finally, we have the three-gluon vertex corrections,

\displaystyle e\,\Gamma_{qq\gamma}^{(2,3V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_3V_fig2}

\displaystyle\qquad=-ie\,g_{s}^{4}\,\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)

(271)

where again we split the vertex function into separate contributions as
V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}=V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1}+V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{2}
where,

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1}&=\frac{W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+q+l,-l,k-l)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(272)

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{2}&=\frac{\gamma_{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,l,k)}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(273)

which has the same structure as eqs. (
269
) and (
270
).

Next, the uncrossed ladder contributions to the two-loop
qcd
corrections to the quark-antiquark-gluon vertex, eq. (
68
) are given by,

\displaystyle g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,UL)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL_fig3}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL_fig1}

\displaystyle\quad=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left[C_{F}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\left(V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}+V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}\right)+\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)^{2}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{3}\right]\,,

(274)

where the kinematic functions
V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{i}
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, are defined in eqs. (
264
)-(
266
). The crossed ladder diagrams are

\displaystyle g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,XL)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL_fig3}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL_fig1}

\displaystyle\qquad=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left[\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)^{2}\left(V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1}+V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}\right)\right.

(275)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\left(C_{F}-C_{A}\right)\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{3}\right]\,,

where
V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{i}
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, are defined in eqs. (
268
)-(
270
). Similarly,

\displaystyle g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,3V)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_3V_fig2}

\displaystyle\qquad=-ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\left(V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1}+V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{2}\right)\,,

(276)

with
V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1}
and
V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{2}
defined in eqs. (
273
) and (
272
).

If the external particle is a gluon, we have four additional topologies compared to the electroweak case. The four-gluon vertex contribution is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,4V)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_4V}=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,W^{(2,4V)\,\mu}\,,\end{split}

(277)

with

\displaystyle W^{(2,4V)\,\mu}=-\frac{\gamma_{\nu}(\not{p}+\not{k}+\not{q})\gamma_{\sigma}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\rho}\left[g^{\mu\rho}g^{\nu\sigma}+g^{\mu\nu}g^{\rho\sigma}-2g^{\mu\sigma}g^{\nu\rho}\right]}{l^{2}k^{2}(p+l)^{2}(l-k-q)^{2}(p+k+q)^{2}}\,.

(278)

The case where the external gluon connects to an internal gluon line of the uncrossed ladder diagram, shown in eq. (
256
), is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,UL-3V)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_fig2}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left[\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\,W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}+\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}\right]\,.\end{split}

(279)

Analogous to eq. (
32
) it is useful to decompose
W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}
into a scalar contribution
Q^{(2,UL)\,\mu}
and a contribution associated to ghosts
O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}
as follows,

\displaystyle W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)=Q^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)+O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)\,,

(280)

with

\displaystyle Q^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=2(1-\epsilon)\frac{(q-2l)^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})}{l^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\,,

(281)

\displaystyle O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{l^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}(l-q)^{2}}\left[\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})(\not{l}-2\not{q})\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+(\not{l}+\not{q})(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma^{\mu}\right]

(282)

where
S^{(1)}
is defined in eq. (
36
).

Similarly,
W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}
can be written in terms of the one-loop function
W^{(1)\,\mu}
, defined in eq. (
32
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)=-\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})W^{(1)\,\mu}(p+l,q,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(283)

Again, we can write,

\displaystyle W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)=Q^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)+O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)\,,

(284)

where
Q^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}
and
O^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2}
are obtained by replacing
W^{(1)\,\mu}
in eq. (
283
) by the one-loop scalar and ghost parts defined in eqs. (
33
) and (
34
), respectively.

Next, we have the crossed-ladder-type diagrams with a three-gluon vertex,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,XL-3V)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_fig2}\\
&\qquad=-ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\,\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)W^{(2,XL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)\,.\end{split}

(285)

with
W^{(2,XL)\,\mu}\equiv W^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1}+W^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}
. Again, we separate scalar and ghost contributions from the kinematic parts as

\displaystyle W^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)=Q^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)+O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)\,,\quad i\in\{1,2\}\,,

(286)

where

\displaystyle Q^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(q-2l)^{\mu}V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+l,q-l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\,,

(287)

\displaystyle O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\left[(l+q)_{\alpha}V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+l,q-l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma^{\mu}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+V^{(1)\,\mu}(p+l,q-l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})(\not{l}-2\not{q})\right]\,,

(288)

and

\displaystyle Q^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(q-2l)^{\mu}\gamma_{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l})V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,l,k)}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\,,

(289)

\displaystyle O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{l^{2}(l-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\left[\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})V^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,l,k)(l-2q)_{\alpha}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+(\not{l}+\not{q})(\not{p}+\not{l})V^{(1)\,\mu}(p,l,k)\right]\,.

(290)

Above, we have used the one-loop vertex function
V^{(1)\,\mu}
defined in eq. (
29
). In the coming sections, we will often use the shorthand
X^{(2,XL)\,\mu}=X_{1}^{(2,XL)\,\mu}+X_{2}^{(2,XL)\,\mu}
also for the ghost and scalar contributions,
X\in\{Q,O\}
.

The last topology contains two three-gluon vertices and has the following contributions,

\displaystyle g_{s}\,\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,d3V)\,\mu,c}=\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=79.34853pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_fig3}

\displaystyle\qquad=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,W^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)\,.

(291)

where
W^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}=W^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{1}+W^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{2}
is the sum of the first two diagrams shown on the right-hand side of eq. (
291
).
The third diagram has vanishing colour factor. We decompose the remaining contributions as usual into scalar and ghost parts,

\displaystyle W^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)=Q^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)+O^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{i}(p,q,k,l)\,,\quad i\in\{1,2\}\,,

(292)

with

\displaystyle Q^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(q-2k)^{\mu}\gamma_{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{k})W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,k,l)}{k^{2}(k-q)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\,,

(293)

\displaystyle O^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{1}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{k^{2}(k-q)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\left[\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{k})W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p,k,l)(k-2q)_{\alpha}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+(\not{k}+\not{q})(\not{p}+\not{k})W^{(1)\,\mu}(p,k,l)\right]\,,

(294)

and

\displaystyle Q^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(q-2k)^{\mu}W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+k,q-k,l+q-k)(\not{p}+\not{k})\gamma_{\alpha}}{k^{2}(k-q)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\,,

(295)

\displaystyle O^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}_{2}(p,q,k,l)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{k^{2}(k-q)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\left[W^{(1)\,\mu}(p+k,q-k,l+q-k)(\not{p}+\not{k})(\not{k}-2\not{q})\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+(k+q)_{\alpha}W^{(1)\,\alpha}(p+k,q-k,l+q-k)(\not{p}+\not{k})\gamma^{\mu}\right]\,,

(296)

Again, we will often write
X^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}=X_{1}^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}+X_{2}^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}
also for the ghost and scalar contributions,
X\in\{Q,O\}
.

Appendix D
Three-loop shift counterterms

In this appendix we provide additional technical information about shift counterterms, discussed in section
7
, and provide counterterms that remove shift-integrable contributions from the form factor counterterterms.

D.1
One-loop shift integrability for the one-loop quark jet function

In this appendix, we prove integrability in the
l\,||\,p_{1}
limit of the set of integrands shown in fig.
10
, equal to the following sum of collinear insertions,

\displaystyle\sum_{\text{insertions}}\includegraphics[width=48.29979pt,valign={c}]{l_p1}\otimes\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_NL_A_diag1_n}\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_1_n}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_2_n}

\displaystyle\qquad+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_3_n}

(297)

Here, whenever a gluon with longitudinal polarisations flows into an (off-shell) tree-level sub-amplitude, it bisects the set of outgoing photon momenta as follows,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M0_partition_coll_diag1}=\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{M0_partition_coll_diag3}+\sum_{s=i}^{j}\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{M0_partition_coll_diag2}

\displaystyle\qquad=t^{c}\,\frac{i}{\not{\bar{p}}}(-l_{\mu})\Bigg[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(p,\bar{p},l;\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{j}\})\frac{\not{p}-\not{l}}{(p-l)^{2}}\gamma^{\mu}

(298)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{s=i}^{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{s}(p,\bar{p},l;\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{s}\},\{q_{s+1},\ldots,q_{j}\})\Bigg]\frac{i}{\not{{p}}}

\displaystyle\qquad t^{c}\Bigg[\frac{\not{l}-\not{\bar{p}}}{(l-\bar{p})^{2}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{s}(p,\bar{p},l;\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{j}\})\,\frac{i}{\not{p}}-\frac{i}{\not{\bar{p}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{s}(p,\bar{p},l;\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{j}\})\frac{\not{{p}}-\not{l}}{(p-l)^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{M0_partition_coll_diag5}-\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{M0_partition_coll_diag4}\,.

Repeated applications of the abelian-type Ward identity, eq. (
15
), results in pairwise cancellation of all terms except two, shown graphically on the last line. The first
term vanishes if
\bar{p}\equiv p_{2}

due to the Dirac equation, c.f. eq. (
17
), corresponding to an insertion directly on the incoming antiquark
leg. Applying eqs. (
298
) and the QCD Ward identity for triple-gluon vertices, eq. (
18
), to the graphs on the right-hand side of eq. (
297
) yields,

\displaystyle\sum_{\text{insertions}}\includegraphics[width=48.29979pt,valign={c}]{l_p1}\otimes\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_NL_A_diag1_n}=-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_1_n_ward}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_2_n_ward_1}

\displaystyle\quad-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_2_n_ward_2}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_3_n_ward_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{M1_l_p1_diag1_3_n_ward_2}+\text{ghosts}\,.

(299)

Colour factors are shown below each diagram for convenience. The first diagram after the equality cancels against the contribution of the second diagram proportional to
C_{F}^{2}
. On the second line, the part of the first diagram proportional to
C_{A}C_{F}/2
cancels against the second diagram (insertion on the gluon line). The remainder consists of 1) an integrand proportional to
C_{F}^{2}
, which contributes to the singularity but is factorised from the hard one-loop sub-diagram, and 2) an integrand proportional to
C_{A}C_{F}/2
contributing to both the second diagram on the second line and the last graph after the equality, which cancel locally up to a loop momentum shift
q\to q+l
of the gluon line.

We can easily repeat the analysis above for the remaining integrands contributing to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(1,C)}
, defined below eq. (
105
). This yields the shift counterterm shown in eq. (
107
).

D.2
Evaluation of shift integrands

As an example, the first term in eq. (
112
) is written explicitly as,

\displaystyle=-g_{s}^{4}\,\frac{1}{k^{2}(q-l)^{2}}\,\frac{-ig_{\mu\nu}}{l^{2}}\,\bar{v}(p_{2})\,\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}(\xi_{1};-p_{2}+l+{Q_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}},p_{2},l)

\displaystyle\times\gamma^{\beta}{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{n_{12}+1,n_{123}}(p_{1}+q-{Q_{1,n_{12}}},-p_{1}-q+{Q_{1,n_{123}}})\gamma_{\beta}

\displaystyle\times{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)\,\alpha}_{n_{1}+1,n_{12}}(p_{1}+l+k-{Q_{1,n_{1}}},-p_{1}-l+{Q_{1,n_{12}}},k)\gamma_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{M}_{1,n_{1}}^{(0)}(p_{1}+l,-p_{1}-l+{Q_{1,n_{1}}})\gamma_{\mu}u(p_{1})\,,

(300)

where
n_{i}
denote the number of electroweak vertices at each tree-level sub-graph, with
n_{i\ldots j}\equiv n_{i}+\ldots+n_{j}
. Here,
0\leq n_{1},n_{3}
and
1\leq n_{2},n_{4}
, where
n_{1234}\equiv n\geq 2
is the total number of outgoing photons. On the first and second lines we have used momentum conservation,

\displaystyle p_{1}+p_{2}=Q_{1,n}\,.

(301)

To improve readability, we have used the shorthand,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})\equiv{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(p,\bar{p};\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{j}\})\,,

(302)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p},k)&\equiv\sum_{s=i}^{j}{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{s}(p,\bar{p},k;\{q_{i},\ldots,q_{s}\},\{q_{s+1},\ldots,q_{j}\})\\
&+{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})\frac{\not{p}-\not{k}}{(p-k)^{2}}\gamma^{\mu}\,.\end{split}

(303)

Here, the functions
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})
and
\mathcal{M}^{(0)\,\mu}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p},k)
are defined to include the adjacent (off-shell) fermion propagators,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})\equiv iS_{0}(-\bar{p})\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})iS_{0}(p)\,,

(304)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(0)\,\mu}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p},k)\equiv iS_{0}(-\bar{p})\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p},k)iS_{0}(p)\,,

(305)

where
S_{0}
denotes the fermion propagator defined in eq. (
15
) and we set
iS_{0}(p)=1
(
iS_{0}(-\bar{p})=1
) if
p^{2}=0
(
\bar{p}^{2}=0
) is on-shell. As a tree-level subgraph,
\mathcal{M}^{(0)}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})
may contain no electroweak vertices, for which

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{i,j}(p,\bar{p})\equiv iS_{0}(p)\,,\qquad Q_{i,j}\equiv q_{i}\,,\quad\text{if }j<i\,,

(306)

The amplitude
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}
on the first line of eq. (
300
) includes the graph where
l
is adjacent to the incoming antiquark, and is regularised according to the prescription in eq. (
108
) to avoid double-counting in different collinear regions,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}(\xi_{1};-p_{2}+l+{Q_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}},p_{2},l)\\
&\quad=\gamma^{\mu}\bar{S}_{0}(l-p_{2};p_{1},\xi_{1}){{\mathcal{M}}}^{(0)}_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}(-p_{2}+l+{Q_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}},p_{2},l)\\
&\quad+{{\mathcal{M}}}^{(0)\,\mu}_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}-1}(-p_{2}+l+{Q_{n_{123}+1,n_{1234}}},p_{2},l)\,.\end{split}

(307)

In eq. (
300
) the gluon propagator
-ig_{\mu\nu}/l^{2}
is replaced by the approximation in eq. (
12
) in the collinear region
(1_{l},H_{k},H_{q})
.

D.3
Form factor shift counterterms

The relevant shift counterterm
\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}
for the three-loop form factor follow straightforwardly from the results of
section
7
.
We write the form factor shift counterterm as a sum of six terms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l;\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})\\
&\qquad=\sum_{X}\,\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,X)}(\xi_{1};p_{1},p_{2},q,k,l,\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\})\,,\quad X\in\{\mathcal{J},UL,XL,3V,\Delta,O\}\,.\end{split}

(308)

which follows the assignment explained after eq. (
103
).
Again, it suffices to provide the counterterms valid for the region
(1_{l},H_{q},H_{k})
. To avoid spurious contributions to overlapping collinear regions from a pinched fermion propagator adjacent to the incoming antiquark
p_{2}
, we implement the prescriptions (and use the graphical notation introduced) in eqs. (
108
) and (
153
). We remark that eq. (
308
) requires local ultraviolet regularisation. The corresponding counterterms can be obtained straightforwardly using the method discussed in section
6
.

As in eq. (
103
), the contribution
\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,\mathcal{J})}
removes one-loop shift-integrable contributions from integrands with a one-loop jet subgraph,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,\mathcal{J})}=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_jet_FF_diag_1}-(q\to q+l)\,.

(309)

Here and below, we have suppressed the kinematic dependence for legibility. The square vertex in the graphical representation denotes the hard-scattering vertex (in this case, the Born amplitude) enclosed by a pair of Dirac projectors, eq. (
9
). The contribution to the
(UL)
,
(XL)
and
(3V)
sub-topologies read,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,UL)}=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_FF_diag_1}-(q\to q+l)\Bigg)+\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_FF_diag_3}\\
&\quad+\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\left(1-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\right)\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_UL_FF_diag_2}\Bigg)-(k\to k+l)\Bigg]\,,\end{split}

(310)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,XL)}=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_FF_diag_1}-(q\to q+l)\Bigg)+\Bigg\{\Bigg[\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_FF_diag_2}

\displaystyle\quad-C_{A}^{2}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_XL_FF_diag_3}-(k\to k+l)\Bigg\}\,,

(311)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,3V)}=\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg(\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_3V_FF_diag_1}+\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_3V_FF_diag_2}-(q\to q+l,k\to k-q)\Bigg)\,,

(312)

respectively. Next, we have two-loop shift integrable terms due to gluon self-energy and vertex corrections, the form factor analogue of eq. (
167
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,\Delta)}=ig_{s}^{2}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\frac{\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}}{(q-k)^{2}(l-k)^{2}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_Delta_FF_diag_1}-(k\to k+l,\,q\to q+l)\,.

(313)

Finally, we subtract ghost shift contributions using the following counterterm,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\text{shift},1}^{(3,O)}=\Biggl\{\Biggl[\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_O_FF_diag_3}-\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_O_FF_diag_2}-\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_O_FF_diag_4}\Biggr]-(k\to k+q)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad-\Biggl(\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_O_FF_diag_1}-(k\to k+l)\Biggr)+\Biggl(\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{shift_O_FF_diag_5}-(k\to l-q-k)\Biggr)\,.

(314)

Appendix E
Ghost contributions to the QCD Ward identity for two-loop subgraphs

In this appendix derive the ghost contributions to the QCD Ward identity for two-loop corrections (excluding gluon-self-energy subgraphs) to the fermion two-point function, eq. (
224
).

We begin by contracting the ghost terms for graphs without ladder structure, eq. (
222
), with a longitudinal polarised gluon momentum
q_{\mu}
,
obtaining,

\displaystyle g_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\,t^{c}\,\frac{i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}\,q_{\mu}O^{(2,NL-3V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)\,\frac{i}{\not{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}+\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}-\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_1}-\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_1}+\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_5_1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\includegraphics[width=103.50105pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_6_2}\,.

(315)

The right-hand side is obtained directly from the one-loop ghost identities, eqs. (
45
), (
46
) and (
47
).

Next, we examine ghost contributions from the uncrossed-ladder topology, defined in eq. (
279
). The external gluon with loop momentum
q
may either attach to the “outer” or “inner” loop, yielding different colour factors. For the former we obtain the relation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\,q_{\mu}O_{1}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}=\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}\\

(316)

Similarly, for a three-gluon vertex on the “inner” loop we have,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left(\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}-\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\right)q_{\mu}O_{2}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}=\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_UL-3V_ghost_identity_diag2_alt}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_UL-3V_ghost_identity_diag1_alt}\\
&\qquad+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}\,.\end{split}

(317)

The first two graphs are obtained by straightforward application of eqs. (
46
) and (
47
). We notice that the part of the first line proportional to
C_{A}C_{F}/2
cancels against the first and fourth graphs on the second line of eq. (
315
). The graphs on the second line will be completely cancelled against contributions from the crossed ladder topology, as we will show next.

The crossed ladder graphs with a three-gluon vertex, defined in eq. (
285
), yield the ghost identity,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left(\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}-\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\right)q_{\mu}O^{(2,XL)\,\mu}=\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}\\
&\qquad+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}\\
&\qquad-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}\,.\end{split}

(318)

Examining the diagrams closely, we notice that the
C_{A}C_{F}/2
part of the first graphs on the first and third lines of eq. (
318
) cancel against the second and fourth graphs of the uncrossed ladder topology in eq. (
316
), respectively. Similarly, the first diagram on the second line and the last diagram on the third line of eq. (
318
) cancel completely against the diagrams on the second line of eq. (
317
). Comparing the remaining contributions against the
(NL-3V)
topology, we see that the
C_{A}C_{F}/2
coefficient of the second graph on the first line as well as the third graph on the second line of eq. (
318
) (where the fermion propagator
1/(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{k})^{2}
is cancelled by the Ward identity) vanish respectively against the first graphs on the third and last lines of eq. (
315
).

Lastly, we take a look at the ghost contributions to the two-loop Ward identity for the
(d3V)
topology of the QCD vertex, defined in eq. (
291
), which take the following form,

\displaystyle ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,q_{\mu}O_{1}^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}=\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_2}\,.

(319)

and

\displaystyle ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,q_{\mu}O_{2}^{(2,d3V)\,\mu}=\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1}

\displaystyle\qquad-\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_2}\,.

(320)

We observe that the third graph on the second line and the first graph on the third line in eq. (
319
) as well as the second graph on the second line and last graph on the third line in eq. (
320
) satisfy,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_2}\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag1}\\
&\qquad=ig_{s}^{5}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\,t^{c}\,\frac{-i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}\,\frac{\not{k}(\not{p}+\not{k})\not{k}}{l^{2}k^{2}(k-q)^{2}(l-k)^{2}(p+k)^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{\not{p}}\,,\end{split}

(321)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_3_1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_2}\equiv\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag2}\\
&\qquad=-ig_{s}^{5}\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}t^{c}\frac{-i}{\not{p}+\not{q}}\,\frac{(\not{q}-\not{l})(\not{p}+\not{l})(\not{q}-\not{l})}{l^{2}k^{2}(l-q)^{2}(l-k-q)^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\,\frac{-i}{\not{p}}\,.\end{split}

(322)

In analogy to the one-loop ghost identity of eq. (
46
), the convention is that we symmetrise over the loop momentum routing of the ghost self-energy subgraph in the pictorial representation on the right-hand sides. Again, we apply the abelian-type Ward identity for collinear insertions on the quark line to obtain,

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag2}=\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag2_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag2_2}\,,

(323)

and

\displaystyle\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag1}=\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag1_1}-\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_2L_d3V_ghost_identity_diag1_2}\,.

(324)

We denote the graphs where the “internal” fermion propagator is cancelled, either
1/(\not{p}+\not{k})
or
1/(\not{p}+\not{l})
, by
O^{(2)\,c}_{NF,2}
. The corresponding integrand, defined in eq. (
233
), contains loop polarisation terms that are an impediment to local factorisation and therefore the
ffs
method at higher orders, c.f. the discussion at the end of sec.
10
.

Finally, we compare all remaining graphs with the colour coefficient
C_{A}^{2}/4
. The third graph on the first line of eq. (
319
) cancels identically against the part of the third crossed ladder graph on the second line of eq. (
318
) proportional to
C_{A}^{2}/4
. Likewise, the first graph on the second line of eq. (

) cancels the
C_{A}^{2}/4
part of the second crossed ladder graph on the first line of eq. (
318
).

Additionally, for the first time to three-loop order, we also have a shift propagator for the ghost contributions to the QCD Ward identity. This shift term is the sum of the first graph on the second line in eq. (
319
), the third graph on the first line of eq. (
320
), and the
C_{A}^{2}/4
part of the first crossed ladder graphs on the first and third lines of eq. (

). This combination yields eq. (
225
), represented in terms of planar graphs multiplied by the non-standard colour factor
C_{A}/(2C_{F})
.

All graphs in eqs. (

) - (
324
) where an external quark propagator is cancelled, either
1/\not{p}
or

, enter the definition of
\mathcal{O}^{(2)\,c}_{2}

). Explicitly, we have,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{(2)\,c}_{2}(p,q,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=65.55084pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1_v2}-\includegraphics[width=65.55084pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_2_v2}+\includegraphics[width=100.04768pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_5_1_v2}

\displaystyle\quad-\includegraphics[width=100.04768pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_NL-3V_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_4_1_v2}+\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1_v2}-\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_UL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2_v2}

\displaystyle\quad+\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O2_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_1_v2}-\includegraphics[width=72.45232pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_XL-3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_2_2_v2}+\includegraphics[width=68.99895pt,valign={c}]{qqg_QCD_2L_d3V_O1_ward_ghost_RHS_diag_1_1_v2}

The remaining, uncancelled graphs combine with ghost contributions to the two-loop Ward identity involving the gluon three-point function. This yields the term

Appendix F
Two-loop UV counterterms

In this appendix we provide explicit expressions for the single- and double-UV counterterms of two-loop subgraphs of the three-loop electroweak amplitude.

F.1
uv
sub-divergence counterterms for two-loop subgraphs

In this section, we provide the counterterms that remove divergences in the single-
uv
regions
(H_{k\to\infty},H_{l})
and
(H_{k},H_{l\to\infty})
directly at the integrand level
12
12

12

In what follows the “internal” loop momentum
k
and
l
flow through gluon lines, while
q
denotes the momentum of an external off-shell gluon or photon.
. As we will see below, their integrands can be written in terms of the one-loop counterterms of section
4.3
.
It is easy to verify that the approximations in the single-UV regions defined here satisfy the single-UV analog of the QCD Ward identity, eq. (
71
).

We begin with the single-UV approximations of the electroweak vertex. The uncrossed ladder contributions are given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&e\,\Gamma_{qq\gamma\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,UL)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)=ie\,g_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}^{2}\,\sum_{i=1}^{3}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{i\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\\
&\qquad\equiv\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_sUV_UL_fig2}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_sUV_UL_fig3}+\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_sUV_UL_fig1}\,.\end{split}

(326)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\\
&\qquad\qquad=-\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})S^{(1)}_{\text{UV}}(p+l+q,k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}((p+l+q)^{2})^{2}}\,,\\
&V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)=-\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})S^{(1)}_{\text{UV}}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,,\\
&V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{3\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)=\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})V^{(1)\,\mu}_{\text{UV}}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(327)

Here, we have simply replaced the one-loop functions
V^{(1)\,\mu}
and
S^{(1)}
in eqs. (
264
) and (
265
) with their
uv
-expansions,
V^{(1)\,\mu}_{\text{UV}}
and
S^{(1)}_{\text{UV}}
defined in eqs. (
51
) and (
58
). We remark that by simplifying numerators we could find alternative expressions that are equivalent up to terms which are finite in the UV limits. However, this spoils the application of the one-loop Ward identities, and therefore local factorisation in the limit where
q
becomes collinear to an external fermion.

It is straightforward to see that the crossed ladder and three-gluon vertex contributions to the single-UV limits combine to,

\displaystyle e\,\Gamma_{qq\gamma,\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,XL+3V)\,\mu}\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qqEW_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_fig2}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\qquad=-ie\,g_{s}^{2}\,2C_{F}\,\frac{\Gamma_{qqg,\text{ UV}}^{(1)\,\alpha}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}}\,,\end{split}

(328)

where the UV counterterm
\Gamma_{qqg,\text{ UV}}^{(1)\,\alpha}
for the one-loop QCD vertex was defined in eq. (
55
). Here, the superscript
(XL+3V)
denotes the sum of crossed ladder and three-gluon vertex contributions to the elctroweak vertex. We note that the third crossed ladder diagram shown in eq. (
267
) does not contribute to the single-UV region, as can be shown by simple power counting that the corresponding vertex function
V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{3}
, defined in eq. (
270
), is finite when either loop momentum becomes large.

The single-
uv
approximations of the two-loop self-energy corrections contributing to
\Pi_{qq}^{(2)}
can be written in terms of the UV-approximated vertex functions, up to terms that vanish upon integration. This is in analogy to the one-loop identity eq. (
59
) for
S^{(1)}_{\text{UV}}
. The counterterm for the uncrossed ladder diagram reads,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,UL)}(p,k,l)=\includegraphics[width=93.15147pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_sUV_UL}=ig_{s}^{4}\,C_{F}^{2}\,S^{(2,UL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(329)

with

\displaystyle S^{(2,UL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)=2(1-\epsilon)\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l})S_{\text{UV}}^{(1)}(p+l,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma^{\alpha}}{l^{2}((p+l)^{2})^{2}}

(330)

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\frac{2(1-\epsilon)}{(k^{2}-M^{2})^{2}}\frac{\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\not{k}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\mu}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}+(p+l+q)_{\mu}\sum_{i=1}^{3}V^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{i\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\,.

The first term after the second equality diverges linearly in the UV region where
k
is large, but again corresponds to an odd integral in
k
and therefore vanishes.

The crossed ladder and three-gluon vertex self-energy contributions are singular single-UV region when either gluon loop momentum become large. We can combine them to obtain,

\displaystyle\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,XL+3V)}(p,k,l)\equiv\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_fig1}+\includegraphics[width=86.25pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_fig2_alt}

(331)

\displaystyle\qquad=ig_{s}^{4}\left[C_{F}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)S^{(2,XL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)-\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}S^{(2,3V)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)\right]+(k\leftrightarrow l)\,,

where we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}&S^{(2,XL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)=\frac{V^{(1)\,\alpha}_{\text{UV}}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\equiv(p+l+q)_{\mu}V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\,,\end{split}

(332)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&S^{(2,3V)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p,k,l)=\frac{W^{(1)\,\alpha}_{\text{UV}}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}}\equiv(p+l+q)_{\mu}V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\,.\end{split}

(333)

The vertex functions in the expressions on the second line of eqs. (
332
) and (
333
) are given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\equiv V^{(2,XL)\,\mu}_{2\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)=\frac{V^{(1)\,\alpha}_{\text{UV}}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(334)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)\equiv V^{(2,3V)\,\mu}_{2\text{ single-UV}}(p,q,k,l)&=\frac{W^{(1)\,\alpha}_{\text{UV}}(k)(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\gamma^{\mu}(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(335)

Again, we have simply replaced the one-loop functions
V^{(1)\,\mu}
and
W^{(1)\,\mu}
by their UV-approximations
V^{(1)\,\mu}_{\text{UV}}
and
W^{(1)\,\mu}_{\text{UV}}
, defined in eqs. (
51
) and (
52
), respectively, without simplifying numerators.

We note that together, eqs. (
326
) and (
328
) respect local collinear factorisation by virtue of the abelian one-loop Ward identity, eq. (
15
), and its UV equivalent, eq. (
66
),

\displaystyle q_{\mu}\Gamma_{qq\gamma,\text{ single-UV}}^{(2)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)=\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2)}(p,k,l)-\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2)}(p+q,k,l)\,,

(336)

where
\Gamma_{qq\gamma,\text{ single-UV}}^{(2)\,\mu}
is the sum of eqs. (
326
) and (
328
).

Next, we discuss the single-UV limits of the two-loop corrections to the quark-antiquark-gluon vertex, introduced in appendix
C
.
By simple power counting it is easy to see that the four-gluon vertex contribution
\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,4V)\,\mu}(p,q,k,l)
, defined in eq. (

), is finite when either loop momentum

or

(UL)
,
(XL)
and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{qqg}^{(2,X)\,\mu,c}\to\Gamma_{qqg\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,X)\,\mu,c}\,,\quad X\in\{UL-3V,XL-3V,d3V\}\,,

(337)

by replacing the one-loop functions
S^{(1)}
,
V^{(1)\,\mu}
and

in the numerators by their UV counterparts

,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Gamma_{qqg\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,UL-3V)\,\mu,c}=ig_{s}^{5}\,t^{c}\left[\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{1\text{ single-UV}}+\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)W^{(2,UL)\,\mu}_{2\text{ single-UV}}\right]\,.\end{split}

(338)

Here,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}S^{(1)}(p+l,k)\sim S^{(1)}_{\text{UV}}(p+l,k)\,,\quad\lim_{k\to\infty}W^{(1)\,\mu}(p+l,q,k)\sim W^{(1)\,\mu}_{\text{UV}}(k)\,,

(339)

with

Similarly, the two-loop shift terms
\Pi^{(2,X)\,\text{shift}}_{qq}
with

, introduced in section

, are singular in the single-UV regions. For the uncrossed ladder contribution, the singularities are subtracted with the counterterm,

\displaystyle\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,UL)\,\text{shift}}(p,q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{4}\,\frac{C_{A}C_{F}}{2}\left[S^{(2,UL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l-q)-S^{(2,UL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-ig_{s}^{2}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\frac{\gamma^{\alpha}(\not{p}+\not{l}+\not{q})\Pi_{qq\,\text{UV}}^{(1)\,\text{shift}}(p+l,q,k)(\not{p}+\not{l})\gamma_{\alpha}}{l^{2}(p+l)^{2}(p+l+q)^{2}}\,,

with

defined in eqs. (

) and (

), respectively. The crossed ladder and triple-gluon vertex contributions yield, respectively,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,XL)\,\text{shift}}(p,q,k,l)=ig_{s}^{4}\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\left[S^{(2,XL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l-q)\right.\\
&\qquad\left.-S^{(2,XL)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l)+(k\leftrightarrow l)\right]\,,\end{split}

(341)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,3V)\,\text{shift}}(p,q,k,l)=-ig_{s}^{4}\,\frac{C_{A}^{2}}{4}\left[S^{(2,3V)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l-q)\right.\\
&\qquad\left.-S^{(2,3V)}_{\text{single-UV}}(p+q,k,l)+(k\leftrightarrow l)\right]\,.\end{split}

(342)

Diagrammatically, we can represent the sum of eqs. (

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,XL+3V)\,\text{shift}}(p,q,k,l)&\equiv\frac{C_{A}}{2C_{F}}\Bigg[\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_shift_fig1}-\includegraphics[width=75.90042pt,valign={c}]{qq_QCD_2L_sUV_XL+3V_shift_fig2}\Bigg]\\
&+(k\leftrightarrow l)\,.\end{split}

(343)

We emphasise that each of the shift counterterms integrates to zero, which also holds in the single-UV (and double-UV) regions,

\displaystyle\int_{k,l}\,\Pi_{qq\text{ single-UV}}^{(2,X)\,\text{shift}}(p,q,k,l)=0\,,\qquad X\in\{UL,XL,3V\}\,.

(344)

As we have explained in section
10
, we obtain additional shift-integrable terms associated to ghosts,
